What are some good CxO names for the new CHIEF?"* · *"Did you have another better suggestion other than a grading officer, a quality assurance officer effectively."* · *"The /home/claude_cio is ready to populate."* (the cto's answer, CTO-496 and CTO-500: the fleet's own machinery is the person-bound backlog, so the new officer owns the infrastructure and the coo stays the one testing officer).

**THE SEAT'S BUSINESS** (the cto, CTO-500): the fleet system itself, as DESIGN-PLAN box-scheduler lays it out (`.github/skills/shared-box/SKILL.md` § 8, `corpus/tests/shared/box_scheduler/STEPS.tsv`, `harness/scripts/test_design_box_scheduler_steps.sh --list`): the bus (`harness/scripts/s4e_msg.sh`: rows that close, retire and escalate themselves, one verb per ruling, the RESUME verb, claim notices by touched paths, a done that holds no lock, the recut verb), the records (MODE capped with its archive, batons capped, the RULES index and the redirect map, a ruling found by its number), the landing (one landing command, generated tables merged by regeneration), hygiene (a background handle, a seat kills only its own, runners' outputs under /tmp), and the wake by hook. Not the grader (the coo), not the instruments and the box budget (the cqo), not the gates (the cao), not the box controller (the coo, steps 3 to 6). The rows: box-scheduler steps 8, 9, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 21, 22, 27, 28, 29, 30. Lon's escalation law binds this seat (CTO-498): a row it cannot finish is escalated to the cto the same sitting.

**Skills:** `/fleet-bus` first (every verb and its law), `/design-plans` (the step harness and the rows), `/shared-box` (the box and its DESIGN section 8), `/gates-and-witnesses` before editing any test or DONE-WHEN; the language skills only for a witness. The bus is a bash script under the harness rules; every verb keeps its one-line answer and its three exit codes.

- **CIO-110 (2026-11-10 16:53 CST, cio) -- CIO-108 CORRECTED ON SWIPL: SWIPL'S BOUNDED RATIONALITY IS A BUDGET OF COMPOUNDS BELOW EACH ARGUMENT OF THE ANSWER'S TOP STRUCTURE, NOT A NESTING DEPTH; READ IN ITS SOURCE AND CUT AS A WITNESS.** The cio, 2026-11-10 16:5x CST, correcting one sentence of CIO-108 the same sitting (CIO-108 marked the swipl rule a reading of 16 lines that owed a witness). The binding's top structure spends nothing. MEASURED on swipl 9.0.4 (witness sw21_bounded_rationality, 21 lines, staged under /home/resources/progress/XSB-INSPECT-WITNESSES), N 3: f(g(h(1),h(1),h(1),h(1))) is stored as f(g(h(1),h(1),A,B)), which no depth rule gives; f(g(h(1),h(1)),g(h(1),h(1))) is whole because the second argument starts a new budget; g(h(i(j(1)))) is whole; f(g(h(i(j(X))),X)) is stored as f(g(h(i(A)),B)). So the two oracles both COUNT, and differ in three things the design of CIO-108 (a) keeps apart by dialect: where the count starts over (XSB: once a template variable, never again; swipl: at every argument of the top structure), what stands in place of the cut term (XSB: its functor over fresh variables, a list cell of two; swipl: one variable), and the restraint goal (brat_undefined; radial_restraint). - **CIO-108 (2026-11-10 16:46 CST, cio) -- BOUNDED RATIONALITY AND UNSAFE NEGATION ON THE SCHEDULER: BOTH RULES ARE READ IN XSB AND CUT FROM THE ORACLE, THE CUT IS ONE GOAL AFTER THE CLAUSE BODY, SWIPL CUTS BY A DIFFERENT RULE, AND NO ROW IS CUT UNTIL THE CTO SAYS HOW THE TABLING PRELUDE READS THE PROGRAM'S DIALECT.** The cio, 2026-11-10 16:4x CST, on Lon's word in the cio's window 16:2x (verbatim: "well, plan your work and work your plan, if you know what you like and like what you know."); this is item 2 of the plan, the two reads CIO-102 left owing (XSB's bounded rationality and u_not/1 for table_tests_test_euv). MEASURED on XSB 5.0.0 (witness xw21_bounded_rationality, 27 lines, staged under /home/resources/progress/XSB-INSPECT-WITNESSES, the ref cut by the plan's protocol): with max_table_answer_size N and the action bounded_rationality, XSB walks each variable of the answer template apart, left to right, with one count that starts at 0 and NEVER steps back when a sub-term closes (emu/tries.c line 947: the push is a bare increment). The binding's own top structure counts 1 and is kept; a top list cell counts 0. An inner structure met with the count at N or more is replaced by its functor over fresh distinct variables, else the count rises by 1 and the walk goes in; an inner list cell raises the count first and is replaced by a cell of two fresh variables when it reaches N. The first replacement in an answer adds one negative delay on brat_undefined (syslib/xsbbrat.P line 13: a table whose one clause is tnot of itself). So with N 3: b(f(f(f(2)))) is stored whole, b(f(f(f(f(2))))) is stored as b(f(f(f(f(A))))) delayed on tnot brat_undefined; f(g(1),g(h(i(j(1))))) is stored as f(g(1),g(h(A))) because the closed g(1) still counts; a shared variable is not kept shared (f(X,g(h(i(X)))) reads f(A,g(h(i(B))))). MEASURED on swipl 9.0.4 (a scratch run of the same cases, 16 lines): swipl cuts by NESTING DEPTH and replaces the compound at depth N plus 2 by ONE variable: b(f(f(f(f(2))))) is stored whole and b(f(f(f(f(A))))) is the conditional answer; f(g(1),g(h(i(j(1))))) reads f(g(1),g(h(i(A)))); f(g(h(i(1))),g(h(i(1)))) is whole. Two walks, one a dialect. MEASURED on XSB (witness xw22_unsafe_negation, 3 lines; syslib/tables.P lines 327 to 335): u_not/1 of a ground goal is tnot/1; of a non-ground goal it succeeds with one positive delay floundered(Goal) per call, floundered/1 being a variant table whose body calls floundered_undefined(a), a table whose one clause is tnot of itself. READ IN SCRIP 679a69764: the scheduler's generator (prelude tabling.pl line 24) runs the body, reads the delay register and inserts the answer skeleton; the skeleton (line 23) is ret over the goal's variables, which is XSB's answer template, so the count per variable has its place; the size check (line 258) acts on the action error alone; the flag reader (by_name_dispatch.c line 10913) already takes bounded_rationality; tabling.pl reads no dialect anywhere; no prelude file defines u_not/1 (prolog_lower.c line 721 names it in the meta table alone). THE DESIGN: (a) one goal in the generator between the body and the read of the delay register, for the plain kind alone: when the two flags say bounded_rationality with an integer N it walks each argument of the skeleton by the dialect's rule into a new skeleton, and when any term was cut it calls tnot of the dialect's restraint goal (brat_undefined under XSB, radial_restraint under swipl); the standing negation road pushes the delay, so there is no new delay form and no C. (b) The restraint goal is a table of the shape undefined/0 already has (tabling.pl lines 329 and 330) with its own clause of the tnot lookup. (c) u_not/1, floundered/1 and floundered_undefined/1 are Prolog in xsb_tables.pl. (d) explain_u_val/3 is the four clauses of syslib/tables.P lines 2231 to 2246 over get_residual_sccs/5 and comes behind the residual graph rows; for test_euv the driver sorts, so only which goals stand in the graph and whether a sign is negative reach the answer. BY SOURCE the users in the package are four programs for bounded_rationality (test_brat_ansdepth, test_brat_ansdepth_i, test_answer_sccs, test_euv) and three for u_not (test_flounder, test_answer_sccs, test_euv). WHAT WAITS: questions 1 and 4 of the cio's telegram of 16:31 (the dialect flag table; a prelude calling a dialect hook) decide how (a) knows its dialect and where (b) and (c) live; the rows are cut on the cto's answer, together with the abort dump and the residual graph. The row this ruling is filed on edits line 39 of tabling.pl and this design edits line 24: neither uses a symbol of the other. (1) A syntax file is written FROM THE LEXER SCRIP READS THE LANGUAGE WITH, so it cannot colour a word the language does not reserve or miss one it does: Rebus from the table kwtab of rebus.l, Pascal from the quoted-word rules of pascal.l, Raku from seven word arrays of rk_syntax.c, Unicon from the table unicon_keywords of icon_lex.c. (2) ONE CENSUS TOOL reads those words from the source (never from a list of its own) and reads each in the syntax file, and reads that the file stays inside the closed set of the format the converter accepts; it is the criterion of the four rows behind it. (3) UNICON GETS NO FILE: Icon's file gains Unicon's words and serves both, because the closed set cannot extend a file and no file with a home is copied; Unicon's own distribution file is read in place. (4) What the cio wrote about each language's comments, strings and numbers is read from its lexer (one claim about Pascal's binary and octal forms was not in pascal.l and is corrected in the row). (5) Raku's regexes and quoting forms are NOT coloured beyond a flat string: a wrong colour is worse than none. The eye's acceptance stays Lon's, in a notebook. - **CIO-105 (2026-11-10 15:59 CST, cio) -- tabling-one-engine AMENDED ON THE CTO'S READ AND RUN: THE TWO ENGINES CANNOT BE MIXED TODAY, SO EVERY KIND CROSSES TO THE SCHEDULER IN ONE LANDING; FOUR STEPS IN A LINE; THREE ROWS SUPERSEDED (CIO-104 amended)** THE CTO'S READ (his message of 15:5x CST, each point read in SCRIP 679a69764 or run): (1) THE SEGMENT'S LIMITS are as the cio read them, with a second reason: a parked segment's saved trail names cells by address and the restore writes through them, so a parked consumer cannot outlive its leader; the monotonic road stays on the lowerer's resume clauses. (2) THE HEADER LEAVES: own is an untrailed store that fails with no header current; a get of a cell nobody owns FAILS; a header in a parked segment is a copy, so an owned cell must be a constant of its generator or a counter along one derivation; each leaf is a call with a registry look-up and two trail syncs, so the cost of owning cells 6 and 9 is two leaf calls and its cure, if the count shows it, is one leaf that owns several cells. (3) HYPOTHESIS (b) OF CIO-104 IS A FACT AND WORSE: RUN against swipl 9.0.4, a plain table whose clause calls an incremental table answers p([1,2,3]) in swipl and raises type_error(list,[2|1]) in SCRIP; the same with a subsumptive callee; an incremental table calling a plain one raises type_error(list,2). No program may mix a plain table with an incremental or subsumptive one in one call chain today. The cto cut no cure, rightly: a cure inside the round engine would be deleted by this plan. WHAT THE CIO HAD WRONG: the plan of CIO-104 moved one kind per step. A chain breaks exactly when its ends sit on different engines, and incremental and monotonic tables call each other in tests green today only because both sit on the round engine; the first step alone would have turned those red and could never have landed. THE RULING. (1) Step 2 is ONE landing that moves incremental, monotonic, lazy, subsumptive and their moded forms together; after it no call enters the round engine. Its row carries every clause: prolog-tabling-every-kind-of-table-is-evaluated-by-the-scheduler-in-one-landing. (2) The plan is four steps in a line, never two open at once (they all edit tabling.pl): the plan lands; every kind crosses; the dynamic call; the deletion. (3) The cto's three programs are the plan's witnesses oe8, oe9, oe10, admitted from his evidence folder by the plan's landing row (not copied into the staging folder: one home). (4) THREE ROWS ARE SUPERSEDED and marked not to be worked, each blocked behind the merged row until removed: the incremental row, the monotonic row, the subsumptive row of CIO-104. The bus refuses a parked state that names no row, and the queue is in its file form since this hour, so the cio asks the coo for the sanctioned way to remove a row that was minted and never started. (5) The design text (skill prolog-tabling reference.md section 5, .github 06d1945f4), the staged step table and the plan row are amended to match. THE LESSON, the cio's: a migration plan is cut by what must be true BETWEEN its steps, not only at its end; the cio asked the cto after minting, and should have asked before.****
- **CIO-104 (2026-11-10 15:52 CST, cio) -- THE DESIGN tabling-one-engine: EVERY TABLE OF EVERY KIND IS EVALUATED BY THE ONE SUSPEND-AND-RESUME SCHEDULER AND THE ROUND ENGINE IS DELETED; A KIND IS WHAT A TABLE REMEMBERS, NEVER HOW IT IS EVALUATED** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 15:3x CST, the cio's window: "now make a design your mother would be proud of."). THE PICK: the debt CIO-102 named and left as a hypothesis. MEASURED at SCRIP 679a69764, mode 3, against swipl 9.0.4 (each program prints a line when a rule body runs; SCRIP's ANSWERS equal swipl's in every case): a plain 3-cycle runs its body 3 times in both; a monotonic table 4 and 4; an INCREMENTAL 3-cycle 3 in swipl and 15 in SCRIP; a SUBSUMPTIVE one 4 and 9; an incremental table re-evaluated after an assert and a retract 10 and 35; an incremental table under negation 7 and 24. THE CAUSE: tabling.pl holds two evaluators. Plain and plain-moded tables run on the scheduler, where a consumer of an incomplete table parks and is resumed, so a body runs once. Incremental, monotonic, lazy, subsumptive and kind-moded tables run on the round engine, which re-runs every body each round and again per phase of an alternating fixpoint. The ruling of 2026-10-25 (the cure is suspension, not re-run) was carried out for the first group only. THE DESIGN, whole, is skill prolog-tabling reference.md section 5 (.github 213ebfaab): FIVE LAWS: (1) one body, one run, in every kind; (2) an edge is written where the read happens, on the reading table's record, with the bindings of that moment; (3) three-valued answers come from delay lists and simplification in every kind; (4) a change never evaluates: it marks, or it runs resume clauses; (5) a dependency on a dynamic predicate is on the CALL that was read (swipl re-runs three tables where SCRIP invalidates all five). WHAT A KIND IS, AFTER: plain remembers nothing; incremental remembers what it read and keeps its old answers while it is evaluated again; monotonic remembers the resume clause and the variables at each read of a growing source (the lowerer already compiles those clauses, and they are already right); subsumptive looks first for a completed subsumer; moded aggregates on entry. About 130 of the prelude's 573 lines go, with header cells 2 to 5. WHAT IS UNTOUCHED: invalidation, the lazy force, the comparison of old and new answers, revalidation, the transaction log: they read the record, not the engine. THE PLAN: seven steps; seven witnesses cut from swipl and staged in /home/resources/progress/TABLING-ONE-ENGINE with the step table; the guards are every SWI tabling file and XsbTests family green today, by count, so no step may lose a case. THE ROWS: the plan's landing (the coo's, FREE: witnesses, table, harness, each witness read under SCRIP); step 2 incremental on the scheduler with the edge at the read (the pattern, its eight edits written out against 679a69764's line numbers); step 3 the dynamic call; step 4 monotonic; step 5 subsumptive; step 6 the deletion with a census of names; step 7 is the cio's own re-cut when 6 lands (CIO-89's position row deleted as not real, CIO-102's limit on round-engine tables struck, table_tests_test_sdg_preds joins its step). NOT IN THE DESIGN, each named in the skill: sharing a subsumer still in progress; keeping parked consumers after completion; the order a completed table returns its answers; an update made from inside an evaluation. THREE HYPOTHESES FROM READING, NOT RUN, each given a witness or a reading that settles it: (a) a monotonic resume record stored when the pass ends has lost the bindings made before the read (witness 7); (b) header cell 1 means a table number to the scheduler and a list of tables to the round engine, so a scheduler table that first calls a round-engine table has its cell overwritten and gets it back only if the callee completes at once (tabling.pl 232, 233, 284); (c) '$incr_inval', the fallback of '$tbl_dchange' when no table reads the changed predicate, invalidates every completed table of every incremental predicate, which section 2 of the skill forbids: not cut, to be read when step 3 lands. ALSO OF THIS SITTING: bus-one-queue is COMPLETE (the coo's acceptance landed, Lon ran his line at 15:39, beanstalkd is stopped and disabled); a stale compiled file of the deleted client may sit in other roots' __pycache__, harmless. THE CIO RAN SCRIP on six small programs to take the measurements above and was stopped by Lon at the seventh run; from that point the cio wrote witnesses and cut their answers from swipl only, and SCRIP's reading of the witnesses is the coo's, in the plan's landing row.****
- **CIO-103 (2026-11-10 15:31 CST, cio) -- THE NOTEBOOK KERNEL'S HALF OF STANDARD INPUT IS CUT AGAINST CTO-584'S ONE-BYTE INTERFACE; ONE QUESTION BACK TO THE CTO (CIO-81's open item closed on the kernel's side)** THE ROW (owner cio, behind the runtime row): jupyter-scrip-kernel-asks-the-front-end-for-standard-input-when-a-cells-program-reads-it. The kernel gives the cell's child an input pipe and a notice descriptor named by SCRIP_STDIN_NOTICE_FD; on a notice byte it publishes the output gathered so far, sends input_request on the stdin socket when the execute request allowed standard input, and writes the reply and a newline to the pipe; when the request did not allow it, it closes the pipe once and the read meets end of file. It ignores SIGPIPE. One arm is added to the kernel's protocol gate (asks and answers; does not ask when not allowed; does not ask when nothing is read). THE QUESTION FOR THE CTO: a pipe is fully buffered, so unless the runtime FLUSHES its standard output before it writes the notice byte, a program's prompt text reaches the kernel after the answer; the cio asks that the runtime row flush standard output and standard error before each notice, as part of the same thirteen lines, or that the cto say where the flush already happens.****
- **CIO-102 (2026-11-10 15:30 CST, cio) -- THE TABLE-INSPECTION DESIGN: XSB'S VIEW OF A TABLE'S LIFE IS SERVED FROM SCRIP'S OWN SCHEDULER RECORD; EIGHT ROWS; WHAT THE TWELVE PRINTER ENTRIES OF CIO-87 NEED, ENTRY BY ENTRY; AND CIO-85'S PREMISE CORRECTED BY CTO-582** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 15:1x CST, the cio's window: "pick something that needs designing and design it."). THE PICK: the largest open tail of step 5 (CIO-87, CIO-89): the XsbTests entries that print what is inside a table. THE DENOMINATOR is the cto's first-cause table (CTO-582, /home/resources/progress/RECUT-XSB-IMPORT-REDS-FIRST-CAUSE.tsv): CIO-85's sentence that the test distribution's module files are not in the container is WRONG (they are vendored under flattened names and staged beside the entry) and no entry is red at an import; a name is a row when an entry's first cause names it. THE FINDING. Every fact XSB's inspection predicates report is a fact of its SLG engine at one moment: how often a table was called (emu/tables.c 149, slginsts_xsb_i.h 455, 539, 549), how many readers still hold a choice into a completed incremental table (tc_insts_xsb_i.h 808 to 866), a table's place and leader among the tables in progress (the completion stack; tr_utils.c 6076, 6309; debug_xsb.c 1384), and who called whom while a table was in progress (tr_utils.c 6357, 6384). SCRIP's suspend-and-resume scheduler (the one-body-one-run engine of xsb-tabling-wfs step 6; rt_pl_dop_tbl_sl_c, by_name_dispatch.c 27922) already keeps the same structure per table in pl_trie_sl_t: depth is the place, low the leader link, next the stack, pcons and ncons the positive and negative consumers. So the design adds no second bookkeeping: the record gains four integers (calls, visitors, creator, negfirst), the leaf five operations (count, visitor in, visitor out, read the record, read the chain), and everything else is Prolog in xsb_tables.pl. No global, no new root, no trace line (the new members are integers after ncons). THE RULES, each read in the oracle's source and then CONFIRMED BY RUNNING XSB 5.0.0 on five witnesses written for it (/home/resources/progress/XSB-INSPECT-WITNESSES, .P beside .ref, cut by the plan's own protocol). (1) CALLS: 1 at creation, plus 1 for each positive call that finds the table, complete or not; tnot of an existing table adds nothing. (2) VISITORS: 1 per reader of a completed incremental table from its first answer until it is backtracked out, cut, or thrown over; still 1 at the last answer; so the reader is a setup_call_cleanup around a goal that is never determinate. (3) LEVEL: walking the tables in progress from the youngest, a table's level is 1 plus the least low seen so far (XSB lowers the leader of every younger frame eagerly; SCRIP lowers only the running generator's, so the view takes the running minimum). (4) EDGES: a table X is affected positively by every table in whose body a positive call met X while X was in progress, its creator included; a table first called by tnot has its creator among the negative edges instead; lists in creation order. (5) ANSWER ORDER: XSB lists a table's answers by its trie, newest child first at every node, so the listing is a pure function of entry order (below 9 children a node: above that XSB hashes). (6) THE ORACLE'S OWN QUIRK KEPT: morph_dep_graph counts the first element of each merged run twice. THE ROWS (owner cio, served to nobody under TRIO; the first two are FREE, the rest wait in the order named): the record row (C, one gate written first); the admission of the five witnesses to plan xsb-tabling-wfs as steps 12 to 16, each red; step 12 the three reads and the count sites; step 13 visitors (closes incremental_tests_test_visitors and its s_ twin); step 14 the tables in progress (print_incomplete_tables, get_sdg_info, print_sdg_info, sdg_scc_info); step 15 the abstracted graph (closes table_tests_test_sdg_print); step 16 table_dump and the listing order. ONE MEASUREMENT ROW for the coo: XSB also RETURNS a completed table's answers in trie order (witness xw20: facts 1, 2, 3 read back as [3,2,1]) where SCRIP returns entry order; the row counts the red entries that differ only in order, and the cio cuts a step or writes that it is not worth one. THE TWELVE ENTRIES. test_visitors and s_test_visitors: step 13. test_sdg_print: step 15. test_table_dump: step 16 and then the order measurement. test_td_incomp: steps 14 and 16 and then the dump an abort writes (get_scc_dumpfile, the flag exception_pre_action): NOT READ YET, owed. test_sdg_preds: step 15, the cto's gensym prepare/1, and then a design the cio owes: its three incremental cases print from inside a recursive incremental table, and incremental tables still run on the round engine, which re-runs a body each round (HYPOTHESIS from reading tabling.pl 101 and 214 to 238, not run; the 2026-10-25 ruling that the cure is suspension was carried out for plain and moded tables only). test_get_idg_info: the cto's reader first (its first cause is a parse error), then get_idg_info over the get_incr_sccs row. test_answer_sccs and test_euv: get_residual_sccs numbers the undefined answers by a search started in address order, so they need the order answers were created across tables; NOT CUT, the emulator's get_residual_sccs is not read yet. test_fl_analyze_scc, test_forest_view, test_forest_view_turnoff: omitted today as NO_ORACLE_ANSWER; the forest log is a trace of XSB's own engine events and is not reproducible short of being that engine; test_fl_analyze_scc only analyses a stored log and could be served by re-expressing about 200 lines of tables.P: left with Lon's standing no-exclusion ruling (CIO-60) as a measured omission, re-read when the rest is green. KNOWN LIMITS written into the rows: tables of the round engine are not on the chain; a subsumed call does not count on its producer; the hash threshold.****

- **CIO-93 (2026-11-10 14:45 CST, cio) -- WHERE A COLLECTION THAT IS NOT HOSTED LIVES: NOTHING ALREADY ON THE BOX IS MOVED; A NEW OR UNPACKED COLLECTION GOES UNDER /home/resources/collections/LANGUAGE/NAME; ONE INDEX NAMES THEM ALL** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 14:4x CST, the cio's window, on CIO-92: "If the LICENSE does not match, do the test harness to work directly from proper /home/resources installation or whatever is a nice folder struture."). So the in-place suite of CIO-92 (2) is his own design and stands. READ: the collections already on the box are named by path in the record (roast-master by 10 harness scripts, 4 corpus files and 7 .github files; jcon-master, Pascal-P5, prolog-demos, pascal-demos by provenance notes in the corpus), and several ARE an oracle's own installation (icon-master, prologs/xsb, FPCSource, rakudo-2026.05, unicon), which the accessors name. THE RULING. (1) NOTHING THAT EXISTS IS MOVED, copied or linked (no duplicate files, no symbolic links): a suite's ALL.source names the collection where it already sits; an oracle's own tree is read in its installation. (2) A collection that arrives as an archive or is fetched new is unpacked ONCE under /home/resources/collections/LANGUAGE/NAME (the first: /home/resources/collections/snobol4/vanilla from vanilla.tar.gz); the archive stays as it is. (3) ONE INDEX, /home/resources/collections/INDEX.tsv: a line a collection read in place or hosted from the box: its name, language, root, licence, class A to D, origin, version, and the corpus package that reads it. The tool row creates it; every mining row adds its line; the layout census reads red a package whose ALL.source names a root the index lacks. (4) /home/resources is not in git, so the index and the unpacked collections are the box's own state: a second box gets them by the origin and version each line records. Two rows are amended by hand to this (the tool row and the Vanilla row).**

THE LICENCES, each read in its own file under /home/resources. CLASS A, PUBLIC DOMAIN, fits CC0 outright: the Icon distribution (README: "This material is in the public domain"), so the Icon library's packs but for a file that states its own terms; Unicon's copy of the Icon library (its COPYING says so). CLASS B, COPYING ALLOWED WITH A NOTICE KEPT (MIT, BSD, Artistic 2.0, a permission notice): CHAT-80 (MIT since 2020, its README), hakank, PRESS, eliza, Trealla, Scryer, SWI-Prolog, the Icon pack itweak, raku-examples, the roast, Rakudo's and NQP's test trees. CLASS C, COPYLEFT (GPL or LGPL): Unicon's uni tree and extensions, Ciao, YAP, XSB, Free Pascal's tree, the Pascal Lex and Yacc, the Lisp in Pascal, the advent-of-code Prolog. CLASS D, RESTRICTED OR UNSTATED: Vanilla SNOBOL4 (copying only of the whole package, unmodified, together), TeX (all rights reserved; a changed copy may not be called TeX), the Icon pack euler ("similar to GNU Copyleft", its terms file absent), and with no licence file at all: Bratko's programs, PrologPuzzles, the provers, the clpfd set, swi-bench, Stanford Pascal, advpas350, Coco/R, DSA, Wirth 1976, the Rosetta Pascal and Prolog drop. THE RULING. (1) THE CIO'S READING OF "FIT": only class A fits CC0. A class B licence lets the corpus carry the files with their notice, as NOTICE already does for five collections, but the files are then NOT CC0 and a reader of the corpus's licence would be misled about them; so class B is NOT hosted on the cio's own word. It is Lon's to say whether a NOTICE entry counts as fitting; until he does, B is read in place like C and D. (2) A SUITE READ IN PLACE: the package folder in the corpus holds only what is ours: the index ALL.csv (its file column the program's path from the collection's root), the answers beside each path cut from the oracle (NAME.out, NAME.err), and ONE new small file, ALL.source: the collection's root under /home/resources, where it came from, the commit or archive it is, and its licence's name. The programs are read from that root when the suite runs; a root that is absent makes the suite REFUSE as could-not-measure, never red. An archive (vanilla.tar.gz) is unpacked once beside itself under /home/resources, not into the corpus. (3) A HOSTED SUITE (class A) is the package form of CIO-59 with the same ALL.source, kept as its provenance. (4) EVERY SUITE BEGINS WITH A CENSUS by the oracle: which programs it runs to the same answer twice with standard input closed, no network, no terminal; those are entries; the rest are listed with the reason (needs input, needs a display, is a library with no main, does not end), named and not dropped silently (CIO-60). (5) THE ROWS: one tool row (the builder and the one grader read ALL.source; SCRIP's package switch is asked of the cto as a step of his plan), then one row a collection, the coo's, in the order: Vanilla; the Icon packs; CHAT-80; raku-examples; PRESS; XSB's examples; Trealla's remainder; hakank's remainder; the small Prolog sets; TeX with tangle; the Pascal sets; then the large trees, each by its own folders (Unicon's uni, Free Pascal's tests, Scryer, Ciao, YAP, Rakudo's t). (6) WHAT THIS RULE SAYS OF PACKAGES ALREADY HOSTED is told to Lon and changed by nobody: xsb_tests (LGPL), fpc_tests (Free Pascal's terms), the Rosetta packages (GNU Free Documentation License), swi_tests (BSD), trealla_tests, logtalk_iso, inriasuite, the puzzles (MIT) are hosted today under NOTICE or under none; read strictly, his sentence would move them to the in-place form too. - **CIO-89 (2026-11-10 14:32 CST, cio) -- THE DECKER ENGINE SCALE IS CUT TO FIVE ROWS FROM THE ENGINE'S OWN SOURCE: TWO COSTS THAT GROW WITH THE NUMBER OF TABLES ARE CURED, ONE TABLE'S MEMORY IS MEASURED, AND THE NESTED EVALUATION'S STACK IS DECLARED, NOT RE-ARCHITECTED (step 5 of Lon's seven; the old row's facts (a), (b), (c))** READ IN SCRIP 08c716738, src/parsers/prolog/prelude/tabling.pl and runtime/unification.c. THE ENGINE AS IT IS: a tabled call that meets a new table pushes it on the leader's list of tables in progress and evaluates it NESTED ('$tbl_enter' 232 to 234, '$tbl_eval1n' 262); a table that read nothing incomplete is completed at once; otherwise the smallest position of what it read decides whether it leads its component, which is then iterated and completed together ('$tbl_nest', '$tbl_srounds', '$tbl_scc', 263 to 270). TWO COSTS FOUND THAT GROW WITH THE TABLE COUNT. (1) Every subsumptive call lists EVERY table and rebuilds each one's goal term ('$tbl_sget' 93: '$trie_entries' of the whole variant table, then subsumes_term on each): quadratic. CURE, two rows: a trie leaf that walks the trie by the goal's own tokens and returns only the entries that subsume it, and the one clause that uses it. (2) Every read of an incomplete table, and every nested evaluation, finds the table's position by taking the length of the list in progress and walking it ('$tbl_pos' 277 to 279). CURE, one row: the table's data term gains a seventh argument, its position given at entry, since the position is only ever compared. MEASURED BY NOBODY YET: what one complete table costs in heap. One row, the coo's reading at 1,000, 5,000 and 10,000 tables. THE STACK: the nesting follows the program's own chain of new tables, as XSB's does; XSB's stacks grow by themselves and were never held to 4 MB when it answered decker, and SCRIP's 4 MB is the default cell of the entry's attribute row, not a measurement. So the entry DECLARES the stack and heap it needs, measured after the two cures (Lon 2026-09-28: every program's necessary stack and heap are stored in its attribute row). Only if no stack up to 256 MB or no time under 600 s passes is the nested evaluation itself re-designed, on that measurement. THE ROWS: prolog-tabling-a-trie-leaf-returns-the-entries-whose-key-subsumes-a-goal-by-walking-the-trie; prolog-tabling-a-subsumptive-call-finds-its-subsuming-table-through-the-trie-walk; prolog-tabling-a-table-carries-its-own-position-in-the-evaluation-so-an-incomplete-read-scans-no-list; prolog-tabling-the-standing-heap-cost-of-one-table-is-measured-at-1000-5000-and-10000-tables; prolog-tabling-the-decker-entry-declares-the-stack-and-heap-its-evaluation-needs-measured-after-the-two-cures. The old engine-scale row stays as their head until the last one reads green. BOTH COSTS ARE READ IN THE SOURCE AND UNMEASURED: how much of decker's 300 seconds each one is, the last row's four readings will say. STEP 5 OF THE SEVEN IS CUT but for seventeen one-entry rows of inspection printers (CIO-87), which follow in order of cost.**

- **CIO-81 (2026-11-10 14:07 CST, cio) -- THE JUPYTER DESIGN IS AUDITED LINE BY LINE AGAINST THE OFFICIAL DOCUMENTS: THREE GAPS IN THE KERNEL BECOME ROWS, TWO OF THE CIO'S ROWS ARE AMENDED, STANDARD INPUT IS AN OPEN DESIGN WITH THE CTO, AND THE HIGHLIGHTERS ARE CONVERTED FROM LON'S SUBLIME SYNTAX FILES** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 14:1x to 14:3x CST, the cio's window: "Have you read through the JupyterLab interface documents line by line and ensured we have a sound design?" The cio's answer was No. Then: "If JupyterLab has HIGHLIGHTERS then we'll want to create ones for our languages." and "I sure hope that Jupyter Lab/Notebook has REGEX-based syntax coloring then you could take the Sublime syntax files as a source."). WHAT WAS READ, each whole: Messaging in Jupyter (the authoritative protocol text, 2,119 lines, specification 5.5), Making kernels for Jupyter (278 lines), JupyterLab's Develop Extensions (the prebuilt extension sections), fetched from their public repositories; the installed JupyterLab 4.5.2's own code for how a language is registered and found; and the five notebooks validated against the notebook format schema with nbformat (all five valid, format 4.0). WHAT HOLDS, read in src/driver/kernel.c at SCRIP 08c716738: the wire form (identities, the delimiter, the HMAC-SHA256 over four parts, checked on receipt), the header's six fields with date and version 5.3, the parent header, busy and idle around every request and starting once, the five sockets, the execute reply in both forms with its counter, the error message, the interrupt by message, shutdown on either socket, and the kernel information reply's fields. THE GAPS, each a row for kernel.c with one new line of the kernel gate: (1) a silent request still broadcasts execute_input, and the counter rises for a request with store_history false; (2) the heartbeat goes unanswered while a cell runs, because the cell's wait loop polls the control socket and not the heartbeat; (3) stop_on_error is never read, so the requests already waiting run after a failed cell. TWO AMENDMENTS of the cio's own rows: the session-language row must NOT write codemirror_mode (JupyterLab uses that key alone when it is present and would force plain text; left out, the language is found by mimetype, name and extension); the completeness row answers unknown for a tag it does not know. NOT GAPS, said so nobody chases them: the 5.5 additions (the welcome message, subshells, the debugger) are not claimed, the kernel says 5.3; history and inspection are optional and stay empty. OPEN, a design with the cto: STANDARD INPUT. Today a cell's stdin is /dev/null, so INPUT, read() and readln end at once; the specification lets a kernel ask the front end for a line (input_request on the stdin socket when the request allows it). A kernel cannot see that its child is blocked reading a pipe, so the runtime must say so: one byte on a side channel before a read that would block, the kernel then asks the front end and writes the line to the child. Where SCRIP reads standard input is the cto's to say; the row is cut when he has. THE HIGHLIGHTERS. (a) Two colourings exist and both follow the kernel's language_info: Pygments for exports (lexers exist on the box for Snobol, Icon, Unicon, Prolog, Delphi for Pascal, Perl6 for Raku; none for Snocone and Rebus) and CodeMirror 6 in the live editor (JupyterLab ships Pascal; nothing for SNOBOL4, Snocone, Rebus, Icon, Unicon, Prolog, Raku). (b) The live editor takes a stream parser, a tokenizer that matches regular expressions with a stack of states: the model of a Sublime syntax file. So ONE converter turns each of Lon's Sublime syntaxes (corpus/editor_configs/sublime: SNOBOL4, Snocone, Icon, Prolog) into data, and ONE generic stream parser interprets that data; a language is then a data file, not a program. The converter refuses anything outside the closed set of Sublime features those four files use. Its row is cut (the coo's). (c) The extension that registers the languages (name, mimetype, extensions, the generic parser over the language's data) is a prebuilt JupyterLab extension: its built folder is copied to share/jupyter/labextensions and needs nothing more on a user's box, but BUILDING it needs Node.js and the jupyter-builder tool, which this box lacks; that install is Lon's line. Its rows (the generic parser and plugin source under corpus/editor_configs/jupyterlab, the build, the copy by the kernel install, a tokenizer test a language) are cut when Node is on the box. (d) Still to write as Sublime syntaxes, the single source: Rebus, Unicon (Icon's plus its additions), Raku; Pascal uses JupyterLab's own mode. (e) The same data can drive Pygments lexers for Snocone and Rebus, a later row.**

WHAT THE CIO HAD WRITTEN: that cell k leaves the state cells 1 to k in order would leave. That is true of a Run All only. THE AMENDMENT. (1) The session's one image holds what the cells left in the order they were EXECUTED: a cell run twice runs twice, a cell run out of document order sees what ran before it, a cell never run has left nothing. So the notebooks in corpus/notebooks and the tool that checks them stand as cut: each is a Run All. (3) The rest of CIO-76 stands (one process, a cell in its tag's language, what can be shared is shared, no replay), and the cto's CTO-580 and CTO-581 are the SCRIP design under it: the session program hosts the one image, a SNOBOL4 cell enters by CODE, a cell of another language by one runtime entry a language that compiles a unit into the live image, cut language by language (Prolog, Icon, Raku, Pascal), and the first step is the static one, every fenced section of a document running in document order (the cto's row from CTO-511, never landed). - **CIO-76 (2026-11-10 13:23 CST, cio) -- A NOTEBOOK SESSION IS ONE SCRIP IMAGE WITH ACCUMULATED MEMORY: EACH CELL, IN WHATEVER LANGUAGE, RUNS THROUGH SCRIP INTO THAT IMAGE, AND THE LANGUAGES SHARE THEIR GLOBAL VARIABLES (CIO-73 (1) widened on Lon's word)** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 13:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "Regarding Jupyter cells being varying languages, each cell runs through scrip in accumulated memory and the languages share global variables."). WHAT STOOD BEFORE IT: CIO-73 (1) made a session "in one language" one program, and the cto's CTO-579, cut on that, made a SNOBOL4 session a SNOBOL4 program that compiles each cell into itself by CODE, a Prolog session a consult and a query, and gave Icon, Unicon, Pascal and Raku no session until a second unit can be compiled into a live image. THE RULING. (1) THE SESSION LAW, restated: a notebook has ONE session, one SCRIP process whose memory accumulates. A cell is in the language its tag names (the first line %%Lang, the cell's metadata, or the kernel's default); cell k leaves the state that cells 1 to k in order would leave as ONE polyglot program, whatever their languages; a global variable one language sets is the same variable to every later cell of any language, as the sections of a Scriptix document share theirs (src/driver/polyglot.c). (2) So a session is not a program of one language and there is not one session a language: the kernel keeps one live image and sends it each cell with its language. A language-specific kernel specification only sets the default tag. (3) What this asks of SCRIP is the thing CTO-579 set aside: a unit of any language compiled into a live image, with the name table shared. That design is the cto's, and it is now the centre of the Jupyter work, not an afterthought. CTO-579's SNOBOL4 road (a cell compiled by CODE into the live program) stands as far as it is the same image every other language's cell enters; a session program that only SNOBOL4 cells can reach does not meet the law. No replay of earlier cells, as before. (4) THE ORACLE for a notebook of one language is unchanged (that language's oracle on the cells in order). A notebook that mixes languages has no outside oracle; its saved outputs are SCRIP's own in both modes, recorded as such, and the notebook is a demo, not a test of the language. (5) The five SNOBOL4 editions are single-language notebooks and go on as CIO-75 ruled.**

- **CIO-73 (2026-11-10 13:13 CST, cio) -- SCRIP IS A COMPLETE JUPYTER KERNEL FOR THE NOTEBOOK AND FOR JUPYTERLAB, AND LON'S FIVE NOTEBOOKS GET A REAL-SNOBOL4 EDITION BESIDE THE PYTHON ONE: THE INVENTORY OF WHAT THE NATIVE KERNEL HAS AND LACKS, THE SESSION LAW, THE STEPS AND WHO CUTS EACH** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 13:2x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "so then you need something to design. I got it, complete the Jupyter notebook and JupyterLab interfacing to SCRIP as its backend. Let's get the 5 IPYNB files which I wrote and placed into snobol4python to be examples of real SNOBOL4 code versus the Python variants."). READ IN THE SOURCE. SCRIP 08c716738, src/driver/kernel.c (617 lines; CTO-512, Lon 2026-11-04: "get rid of the Python and level up"): scrip --kernel CONNECTION_FILE (scrip.c line 2340) binds the five ZeroMQ sockets itself (lines 578 to 606), signs with its own HMAC-SHA256 (18 to 90), answers the heartbeat (566), and dispatches (522 to 552) kernel_info, execute, is_complete, complete, inspect, history, comm_info, interrupt and shutdown; it publishes status, execute_input, stream and error. WHAT IT LACKS, each by its lines: (a) NO SESSION: k_run_cell (397 to 448) writes each cell to a fresh temporary file and runs it as a new scrip process, so a variable, a pattern or a DEFINE of one cell does not exist in the next; (b) output is kept until the process ends and sent once (the loop at 432 to 440 appends, k_execute streams at 483), so a long cell shows nothing while it runs; (c) the cell's stdin is /dev/null (411, 414) and no input_request is ever sent, so INPUT reads end of file; (d) an error reply says only "LANG cell exited N" (491), SCRIP's own message is left in the stderr stream; (e) no execute_result and no display_data, so nothing richer than text can be shown; (f) is_complete always answers complete (534), complete answers no match (535 to 539), inspect answers not found (540), history answers empty (541); (g) kernel_info names one language scrip with file extension .md and plain text for both highlighters (529 to 531), so no cell is coloured and an exported notebook has no lexer; (h) no installer: the two kernel specifications on this box were written by hand, scrip-native naming the binary of one seat's root (/home/claude_cto/SCRIP/scrip) and scrip naming a Python module on a /tmp path, and neither declares interrupt_mode. The gate test_gate_scrip_kernel_speaks_the_jupyter_protocol.sh sends three cells of three languages and reads Hello, World! from each; it tests no state, no input and no error. Jupyter, JupyterLab and the classic notebook are installed for the user (jupyter_client 8.8.0, ipykernel 7.1.0). THE FIVE NOTEBOOKS, read at /home/claude_cto/snobol4python/notebooks (the clone at 8669c58): A_SNOBOL4_Tutorial_in_Python (36 code cells, 57 markdown), SNOBOL4_Green_Book_in_Python (25, 42), Project_XLR8R (18, 18), WHO_went_to_WHERE_looking_for_WHAT (23, 14), DEFINE_statement (11, 12): 113 code cells, about 1040 lines, the Python kernel, the library SNOBOL4python and Python's re module; no cell reads input, none shows rich output, none carries saved output; nearly every cell uses names an earlier cell defined. THE DESIGN. (1) THE SESSION LAW: a notebook session in one language is ONE PROGRAM. Running cell k leaves the state that running cells 1 to k in order as one program would leave, and prints what that program would print for cell k. So the oracle of a notebook is the oracle of the language run on its cells joined in order, and the kernel is right when each cell's output equals that program's output for the cell. How the kernel keeps the state (a live image that compiles each cell into itself, which SNOBOL4's own CODE road already does at run time, or another way) is the cto's to design; replaying every earlier cell at each execution is not acceptable as the end state (side effects repeat and the cost grows with the notebook). (2) THE COMPLETE INTERFACE, the eight lacks above: the session (a); output published as it is produced (b); INPUT served by an input_request when the front end allows it (c); the error reply carrying SCRIP's own message and statement (d); a value shown when the language has one for a cell, and display_data for an image, which is how an Icon window's picture reaches a notebook (e); completion from the language's builtin table and the session's names, an honest is_complete per language, history of the session (f); one kernel specification a language beside the general one, each naming its language to the front end with its file extension and its highlighter (g); an install verb of the binary itself that writes the specifications for the binary that ran it (h). JupyterLab and the classic notebook speak this same protocol; nothing is done twice. (3) THE NOTEBOOKS: each of the five gets a SNOBOL4 edition, the markdown kept, every code cell rewritten as SNOBOL4 that prints what the Python cell prints, its saved output cut from SPITBOL by the session law; where a Python cell shows Python's own regular expressions against the pattern library, the SNOBOL4 edition shows the SNOBOL4 pattern and the markdown says what it replaces. They are written smallest first: DEFINE_statement, Project_XLR8R, WHO_went_to_WHERE, the Green Book, the Tutorial. THEIR HOME: corpus/demos/notebooks/snobol4, graded like any demo by executing the notebook through the kernel and comparing each cell's output with the saved one; whether they are also pushed into the snobol4python repository on GitHub beside the Python ones is Lon's word, since that is a publication outside the four fleet repositories. (4) WHO CUTS: the cto, the session and what the runtime must do for it (a), (b), (c) and the display road (e), which are SCRIP's execution model; the cio, the protocol rows that kernel.c alone decides (d), (f), (g), (h), the acceptance (a notebook executed through the kernel reproduces its saved outputs), and the five notebook rows. The coo codes what touches the harness. (5) DEPENDENCIES BY SYMBOL: the notebook rows wait on the session (a cell that uses an earlier cell's name cannot pass without it); (d), (g) and (h) wait on nothing.**

MEASURED by the cio 13:0x: Xvfb and xvfb-run are on the path (phase 0 (a) is done). READ IN THE ORACLE'S SOURCE, /home/resources/icon-master/src/runtime, every allocation site of the window sources, 42 in all, each with its file, line and class in /home/resources/progress/ICON-GRAPHICS-ALLOC-SITES.tsv: 5 RECORDS (the window, context, binding and display records, through the one macro GRFX_ALLOC of h/grttin.h line 188, which carries the record's type name); 19 buffers and names HELD BY A RECORD (colour tables, labels, font names, the display's colour pointer array; they live as long as their record or until replaced); 6 SCRATCH buffers allocated and freed inside one function (the curve's points, rwindow.r 1486 to 1518; two colour caches, rxwin.ri 2456 to 2476; the median-cut list, rimage.r 702 to 720; two point arrays in fwindow.r); 5 CALL-EXTENT buffers of the GIF reader and writer (rimage.r 112, 272 to 274, 490: file-scope pointers allocated by a helper and freed by the clean-up before the read or write returns); 1 buffer HANDED TO X (rxwin.ri 2497, an image's data, which XDestroyImage would free); 6 sites under WinExtns that an X build does not compile. Also read: no pointer of the layer is given to X to keep (no XSaveContext, no client data); the layer never calls back into emitted code; the collector moves blocks only at a safe point, which is the return of an emitted call into the runtime (skill gc-collector 1.5 and 1.6), so inside one window function nothing moves. THE RULING, a design for the cto to cut, whose memory model and collector these are. (1) THE CONVERSION IS MECHANICAL: the regeneration script, not a hand, turns every allocation name of the generated C into one of three entries by the site's class, and refuses rc 2 a site it cannot class or a class the table does not list; free becomes nothing at every site. (2) SCRATCH AND CALL-EXTENT GO ON THE STACK IN LIFO ORDER (11 sites, 12 with the detached image buffer): each window function's leaf marks the stack region on entry and releases it on return, so everything taken between is released in reverse order by construction; the script proves the flow by checking that no scratch pointer is assigned to a record field or a file-scope variable that outlives the entry (the five GIF pointers are file-scope and are set to NULL by the release). The image buffer joins this class by one patch line that sets im->data to NULL before XDestroyImage. How the region is taken (the machine stack of the leaf's frame, or the spine by the memory model's own LIFO law) and how its size is bounded are the cto's choice. (3) RECORDS AND WHAT THEY HOLD GO ON THE COLLECTED HEAP (24 sites): a record is a heap block whose layout the script generates from its struct declaration in h/graphics.h and h/xwin.h (a field that points to another record or to a buffer of the layer is a heap pointer, a struct descrip is a descriptor, an X handle or a number is raw: the collector's own law of a small static layout); a buffer with no pointer inside is a raw block. Nothing is pinned: the four chain heads and the two palette pointers are roots with a generated layout, and a slid record is re-pointed by the collector like any other block. What the collector needs that it may not have, the cto's to read and cut: a block kind that is a record with a named layout, and a root range that carries a layout. (4) X'S OWN MEMORY STAYS X'S: graphics contexts, fonts, pixmaps and colours are made and freed by X calls, as the source does; they are not ours and are not converted. (5) THE PROOF IS THE COLLECTOR'S OWN INSTRUMENT: the window witnesses run under the stress setting that collects at every safe point and moves every block, so a pointer the layout missed becomes a located fault. (6) PHASE 1 is cut by the cto on this ruling; it no longer waits on a grant.**

All the features."; before it, 08:0x, CIO-35: "I want all the Icon graphics open and restriction lifted. We want all the complete Icon/Unicon."). READ IN THE ORACLE'S SOURCE, Arizona Icon 9.5.25a, /home/resources/icon-master/src: runtime/fwindow.r (2704 lines) declares 58 functions; 48 are the portable facility and 10 (WinPlayMedia, WinButton, WinScrollBar, WinMenuBar, WinEditRegion and the five Win dialogs, lines 2096 to 2702) sit under WinExtns, Microsoft Windows only, and do not exist in an X-windows build. The 48 by group, each with its line in /home/resources/progress/ICON-GRAPHICS-INVENTORY.tsv: attributes and window state 15 (WAttrib, WDefault, WFlush, WSync, Fg, Bg, Font, Clip, Pattern, Raise, Lower, Alert, TextWidth, GotoRC, GotoXY); drawing 15 (DrawArc, DrawCircle, DrawCurve, DrawLine, DrawPoint, DrawPolygon, DrawRectangle, DrawSegment, DrawString, EraseArea, FillArc, FillCircle, FillPolygon, FillRectangle, CopyArea); colours and palettes 7 (Color, ColorValue, FreeColor, NewColor, PaletteChars, PaletteColor, PaletteKey); images and pixels 4 (DrawImage, ReadImage, WriteImage, Pixel); contexts 3 (Clone, Couple, Uncouple); input 4 (Active, Event, Pending, QueryPointer). Three have a result bound that is not zero-or-one: Pixel (a generator), QueryPointer, WAttrib. Beside the functions: 59 window attributes (the table attribs[] of runtime/rwindow.r: ascent bg canvas ceol the four clip values col columns cursor depth descent display displayheight displaywidth drawop dx dy echo fg fheight fillstyle font fwidth gamma geometry height the four icon values image label leading lines linestyle linewidth pattern pointer and its four positions pos posx posy resize reverse row rows size visual width windowlabel x y); 19 keywords (runtime/keyword.r: &window, &x, &y, &row, &col, &interval, &control, &shift, &meta, the nine mouse event keywords &lpress to &rdrag, &resize); the window as a file (open(name,"g") and "x", then write, writes, read, reads, close, flush on it, with a text cursor). The implementation under the functions is two more files: runtime/rwindow.r (1654 lines, device-independent: attributes, colours, palettes, the GIF reader and writer, the drawing helpers) and runtime/rxwin.ri with rxrsc.ri (3241 lines, Xlib), over h/graphics.h (447) and h/xwin.h (193). The README says the material is in the public domain. THE ORACLE ON THIS BOX HAS NONE OF IT: src/h/define.h reads #define NoGraphics 1, and iconx links no X library. The X11 development headers are installed; Xvfb and xvfb-run are not. READ IN SCRIP 08c716738: no line under src names a window function, XOpenDisplay or &window; Icon's builtins are 128 rows of the one builtin table (harness/scripts/builtins.tsv, generated into src/runtime/builtin_table.inc), 119 plain leaves and 9 generators. THE DESIGN. (1) THE AIM is the whole X-windows facility: the 48 functions, the 59 attributes, the 19 keywords and the window file, each answering as iconx built with graphics answers, pixel for pixel on the same display. The 10 Win functions are named and left out because the oracle's own X build lacks them. (2) THREE LAYERS, as the oracle has them. THE DEVICE LAYER is the oracle's own: rwindow.r, rxwin.ri, rxrsc.ri, graphics.h and xwin.h are vendored and compiled, not rewritten, behind one shim header that maps Icon's descriptor and allocation names onto SCRIP's, so a drawing request reaches Xlib by the same calls in the same order and the pixels are equal by construction. THE FUNCTION LAYER is SCRIP's: one leaf a function, written against the device layer from the function's own lines in fwindow.r, one row each in builtins.tsv. THE LANGUAGE LAYER is SCRIP's: the window value (type "window", its image text), open's g and x modes, the 19 keywords, and text input and output on a window. (3) THE GRANT, Lon's to give: the device layer is about 5600 lines of the oracle's C that allocate with malloc and keep tables in globals, which SCRIP's law for hand-written C forbids; the design asks that it live as vendored source in its own directory, exempt from the hand-written rules as a generated table is, linked into the runtime with libX11, changed only through the shim. Without the grant the device layer is a rewrite of those 5600 lines and pixel equality is no longer by construction. (4) THE STEPS AND WHO CUTS EACH. PHASE 0, the box: (a) Lon's line, sudo apt install xvfb; (b) the coo's row icon-oracles-are-built-with-graphics-...: a second build of icont and iconx with graphics beside the first, reached by its own accessor, and the same for Unicon; (c) a graphics test runs both the oracle and SCRIP under a private virtual display, never the desktop's (lib_one_runner.sh already unsets DISPLAY); its answer is stdout plus the image files it writes. PHASE 1, the foundation, the cto's to cut (SCRIP's own files): the vendored device layer and its shim under the grant; the window value and open; the keywords; text input and output on a window; the event queue. PHASE 2, the functions, six groups in the inventory's order, each group cut into rows of at most three leaves by whoever holds the group's files after phase 1, each row naming the function's lines in fwindow.r and its builtins.tsv row; Pixel is a generator row. PHASE 3, the programs: the Icon program library's graphics procedures and its 182 graphics programs, and Unicon's 4, return to the denominator through the exclusion audit rows already minted (tests-exclusion-audit-ipl-..., -unicon-tests-...). (5) DEPENDENCIES ARE BY SYMBOL: every phase 2 row waits on the device layer's header being includable; a drawing row waits on the window value; no row waits on another group. (6) The cio's row icon-graphics-scrip-gains-icons-window-facility-the-58-functions-and-the-window-file-mode stands as this plan's head until phase 1 is cut, then is deleted as an umbrella. Nothing is built by a designer.**

No dependency by likeness of topic, by suite, or by an umbrella; an umbrella row is not a task and is deleted when its parts are cut. WHO: the cio reads the fleet system, the harness, the instruments and the tabling family (CIO-14); the cto reads SCRIP: the pipeline, the runtime, the collector, the memory model and the language front ends; a row that crosses goes to the one whose file it changes. MEASURED 11:4x on corpus 23efe1729 (git ls-files; the class is the first upper-case cell of each row): 92 exclusion files; the EXCLUDED tables hold 2381 classed rows, the ALL.omit files 3565 lines. By class: NO_ORACLE_ANSWER 1357 (24 tables), XSB_EXCLUDED_BY_LON 344 (swi_tests EXCLUDED_CASES), NEEDS_GRAPHICS_FACILITY 186 (ipl 182, unicon_tests 4), THREADS_EXCLUDED_BY_LON 119 (swi_tests 112, trealla_tests 7), S17_CLOSED_BY_LON 99 (roast), NOT_SPITBOL_DIALECT 86, FAKEABLE_NO_OBSERVABLE 47, NOT_A_UNIT_TEST 33, SWIPL_VM_INTERNAL 22, SCRIP_EXCEEDS_ORACLE 18, WINDOWS_ONLY 17, BOUNDED_INTEGERS_ONLY 17, NEEDS_TERMINAL 16, STANDARD_ORDER_BY_VALUE 7, NEEDS_LOADFUNC 4, EXCLUSIVE_BRANCH 4, LON_UNICODE_BY_DESIGN 2, DOTLESS_FLOAT_READ 2, BOUNDED_MAX_ARITY_ONLY 1. The suite table reads swi 2226 of 2311 and xsb 365 of 550. Four queue rows are held skip-xsb-tests, one loadfunc-later, one forget-advpas350. THE RULING. PART ONE, THE EXCLUSIONS. (1) THE LAW: no program and no case is out of a denominator because somebody said so. An exclusion is a measurement and nothing else: the oracle's command, run twice, what each run read, and the date; its class names what was measured, never who decided. (2) VOID AS REASONS from this minute: XSB_EXCLUDED_BY_LON, THREADS_EXCLUDED_BY_LON, S17_CLOSED_BY_LON, LON_UNICODE_BY_DESIGN, NEEDS_GRAPHICS_FACILITY (already lifted, CIO-35) and NEEDS_LOADFUNC: 754 rows. CIO-19's clause that a Lon-keyed class holds though the program passes is void; the hold reasons skip-xsb-tests, loadfunc-later and forget-advpas350 are void and their six rows are re-cut with the others. (3) FRESH EYES ON ALL OF THEM, not the decreed ones alone: every one of the 2381 table rows and 3565 omit lines is measured again by the audit CIO-33 (d) designed, one verdict each: ANSWERED (the oracle gives the same answer twice with the streams apart: the program or case is an entry in the denominator, whatever SCRIP reads on it); NO-ANSWER (the oracle refuses, crashes, times out or differs between its two runs: it stays out, and its row carries the command, both readings and the date); NOT-A-TEST (a library, an include, a data file, a part of a driver: it leaves the exclusion table for the table that names what it is). A released case that needs something SCRIP lacks (threads, XSB tabling under SWI, the window facility, loadfunc, Raku's S17) is ANSWERED and RED, and the red goes to its designer by CIO-14's routing rule; it is not excused. (4) WHAT IT COSTS, told to Lon: if every released case is answered and red, SWI reads 2226 of 2767 (80.4 percent) and the roast 249 of 1401; the true numbers come from the audit, not from this bound. (5) THE ORDER: no board number moves without its measurement. The cio cuts the audit into rows (the audit tool and its record first, then one row a table: the 754 decreed first, then NO_ORACLE_ANSWER, then the rest); the coo, the one testing officer, runs them when Lon lifts the design phase; minted FREE and served to nobody until then (CIO-58). PART TWO, THE ARGUMENTS. (6) CIO-59 (2) is amended: a package's arguments live in ONE file at the package root, ALL.args, in the form of ALL.rc and for its reason (few entries have any): one line an entry that has arguments, the entry's name, then its arguments, each after a tab, so an argument may hold a space; blank lines and lines that begin with # are ignored; an entry not named has none; a line that names an entry and no argument, or an entry the index does not hold, is refused rc 2. There is no NAME.args. Beside the program stand the three files Lon named, NAME.in, NAME.out and NAME.err, and nothing else of ours. SCRIP's package switch reads ALL.args once and NAME.in a test. At a package's conversion ALL.argv (ipl 29 lines, jcon_tests 21, rosetta-prolog 8, aisnobol 1) and the 124 loose NAME.argv files fold into ALL.args, the read-back equal; whether the second cell of ipl's ALL.argv lines is an argument or the program's name is unmeasured and is read before the fold. In corpus/tests the args fence of MULTI.md stands (CIO-34 (2)).**
- **CIO-59 (2026-11-10 11:38 CST, cio) -- THE PACKAGE FORM, ANSWERING THE CTO'S FOUR ASKS OF 11:4x (CTO-575): THE INDEX NAMES THE DISTRIBUTION'S FILE; OUR FILES BESIDE IT ARE NAME.in, NAME.out, NAME.err AND NAME.args; A PER-CASE SUITE'S ENTRY IS ITS FILE AND STAYS ON THE PYTHON ROAD UNTIL THE SHIM LAUNCH IS DESIGNED; THE ROAST IS VENDORED; THE SAME-VERDICT CENSUS OF THE SIX MASTERS IS ASKED NOW** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 11:1x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "We also need major clean up in the $/corpus/packages language folders just like the "tests" folder but they will not have ONE LINERS and MULTI LINERS. Instead the programs will have the same layout as in the distribution from which it came, and side-by-side files with NAME.in, NAME.out, and NAME.err files. And slightly varying ALL files."). MEASURED 11:3x to 11:5x by the cio on corpus 23efe1729 (git ls-files packages, the ALL files left out: 14086 files): files by extension: ref 1484, inp 869, cmp 866, ecp 854, dat 358, std 165, argv 124, in 94, IN 17, out 16, input 10, args 1, err 0. The 16 out files all sit in packages/icon/unicon_tests/ipl/stand, a directory that holds no program, and none has a program of its stem beside it (0 of 16). Eight stems in snobol4/aisnobol hold two program files in one directory (the package's own copy and upstream/). The roast drop is /home/resources/roast-master, 34 MB, 1464 .t files, read in place and not in corpus. The inria index names 445 entries cut from about 64 distribution files and the logtalk index 3617 entries cut from 400 .lgt files (origin column: control/call_1/tests.lgt); the swi index holds 161 files graded by grader swi-cases. THE RULING. (1) THE INDEX. A package's index is ALL.csv at the package root with one more column, file, straight after entry: the program's path from the package root with its extension, exactly as the distribution lays it. entry keeps today's name, so results history, EXCLUDED and PARTITION do not move. A row with an empty file cell is not in package form and the package switch reports it DEFERRED. The cto's dialect column (CTO-575 C) is the second new column. When a package converts, its container of program text retires (ALL.<ext>, ALL.ref, ALL.in, ALL.argv, and ALL.prelude into the dialect), and the stdin and run_args columns with it: the files are the fact. What stays of the ALL set is the index, ALL.omit, ALL.mask and the exit status column. (2) THE FOUR FILES BESIDE THE PROGRAM. NAME is the program's file name less its last extension; beside the program stand NAME.in (stdin), NAME.out (the stdout answer), NAME.err (the stderr answer) and NAME.args (the arguments, one a line, so an argument may hold a space), each absent meaning empty. NAME.args is CIO-34 (3), which stands: Lon's 11:1x word names three files and his 07:5x word made the arguments "another per-program entry"; a file needs no quoting rule and is read by the code that reads the args fence. It is a fourth file and is told to Lon as such; his to overrule into an index cell. SCRIP reads NAME.in and NAME.args and nothing else; NAME.out and NAME.err are read by the Python grader's post step alone. (3) THE DISTRIBUTION'S OWN INPUT AND ANSWER FILES stay where the distribution put them and are never renamed (dat, inp, std, cmp, ecp, input, IN). Where the distribution's input file is the test's stdin, the builder writes NAME.in at admission as a copy and the layout census reads the two equal; SCRIP has one rule and no lookup. The distribution's expected output (std, cmp, ecp) is never the answer: the answer is cut from the oracle (CIO-34 (5)); a census may compare the two and report. (4) A NAME THAT IS TAKEN. Measured today: no distribution file collides with a NAME.out or NAME.err, and eight stems are shared by two programs in aisnobol, where only the package's own copy is an entry. THE RULE: the builder refuses at admission, rc 2, naming both files, an entry whose NAME.in, NAME.out, NAME.err or NAME.args would be a file the distribution ships with another meaning, or would be shared with another entry of the same directory; such a pair is settled by a ruling when the first one is measured, not by a second naming scheme today. (5) THE GENERATED CASES AND THE PER-CASE SUITES ARE ONE CLASS. A case cut out of a distribution file (inria 445, logtalk 3617) is not a file of the distribution, so it is not a package entry; the distribution's file is (about 64 and 400), exactly as an SWI plunit file is (161), and its run yields case lines the grader expands into case rows (CIO-15: the grader column, swi-cases, logtalk-cases, verdict-line). Such a file is launched with a shim before it and a wrapper after it (CIO-21's three files), which the package switch does not do: a row whose grader cell is not empty is DEFERRED by the switch and graded on the Python road until the shim launch has its own design, which is the cio's (CIO-14's routing rule: the grading and plunit shim shape). The end state, a HYPOTHESIS until that design: one index row a distribution file, the case answers as lines of NAME.out, the counts of the suite table unchanged because the grader still counts cases. No generated case is written into corpus/tests as a multi-liner. (6) THE ROAST is a package like the others: its pinned drop is vendored into corpus/packages/raku/roast in the distribution's own layout (1464 files, 34 MB at the source, the size in git unmeasured), file names the .t path, NAME.out beside each. This adds files to corpus and is Lon's yes before a row runs; until then the roast's rows hold an empty file cell and are DEFERRED. (7) THE DRIVERS (a program of ours that calls a distribution's library, DRIVERS.tsv) are not counted here: how many there are and where each stands is measured before the package rows are cut. (8) THE CENSUS. The same-verdict census of the six masters on the cto's regrouped scratch containers (/tmp/s4e/cto/trio) is asked NOW, by one test request from the cto to the coo, because its reading decides the order of the cio's rows: all six the same verdict, and each folder is regrouped FIRST and its loose programs admitted after, straight into format 2 with the file fence (the multi-file programs need it); it is read again at each folder's live regroup as that landing's confirmation (CIO-53's pattern). It is a measurement for the design, not a mint served to the fleet. (9) NOTHING HERE IS BUILT. The cio's package rows are minted FREE and served to nobody (CIO-58); the SCRIP rows are the cto's and park behind Lon's lift.**

- **CIO-34 (2026-11-10 08:00 CST, cio) -- A TEST HAS SIX PARTS, ITS PROGRAM, ITS ARGUMENTS, ITS STDIN, ITS STDOUT, ITS STDERR AND ITS EXIT STATUS; STDERR IS CAPTURED AND ANSWERED APART FROM STDOUT; EVERY ANSWER IS CUT AGAIN FROM THE ORACLE WITH THE STREAMS APART; CIO-24 (h) IS ANSWERED AND CIO-25 (a) AND CIO-32 (3) ARE EXTENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:5x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "So you'll want to re-grade the programs using stdin (INPUT variable) and capturing stdout (OUTPUT variable) and stderr (TERMINAL variable in SNOBOL4). And also programs have command-line arguments, argc/argv. So that is another per-program entry."). MEASURED earlier today on corpus f141ec484: of 16399 table entries 346 are graded with stderr merged into the answer and every other entry's stderr is compared with nothing; arguments live in two places, the index's run_args column and ALL.argv; the exit status lives in ALL.wantrc. THE RULING. (1) THE SIX PARTS of a test: the program; its command-line arguments; its stdin (INPUT in SNOBOL4); its stdout (OUTPUT); its stderr (TERMINAL in SNOBOL4, the error stream in every language); its exit status. Merged stderr retires: Lon's open question of CIO-24 (h) is answered, the streams are apart. (2) IN corpus/tests, a MULTI.md section holds them as fences after its heading, each absent fence meaning empty: the language fence (the program), a fence tagged args with one argument a line (so an argument may hold a space), stdin, stdout, stderr; the exit status stays the index's column. A ONE-liner is a test with no arguments, no stdin, an empty stderr, exit status 0 and one line of stdout; any other test is in MULTI.md. ALL.argv and the run_args column fold into the args fence when a folder converts; the index's stderr column goes. (3) IN A PACKAGE, when its design resumes (CIO-33 b), the parts are files beside the program under its own name: NAME.args, NAME.stdin, NAME.stdout, NAME.stderr, an absent file meaning empty. (4) THE SCRIP RUNNER (CIO-32) reads the args fence and passes the arguments after the two dashes, and always writes NAME.out and NAME.err as two files. (5) THE RE-CUT: every entry's answer is cut again from its language's oracle with the three streams apart: the oracle run with the entry's stdin and arguments, its stdout and stderr captured as two streams, its exit status read, twice, the two runs equal or the entry named UNSTABLE. The fresh stdout and exit status must equal the stored answer; where they differ the entry is named DRIFTED with both, and its answer changes only by that named cut (this is also Lon's "let's not assume" applied to the answers themselves). The fresh stderr is stored as the new part. The 346 merged entries get their two streams from this cut. (6) STDERR IS COMPARED AS STDOUT IS, by the post step: byte for byte, and through the one error voice and the masks where the table declares them, since an engine's own diagnostic (its file name, its line, its wording) is compared by the rules that already compare it on stdout, never byte for byte. BEFORE IT COUNTS, THE MOVEMENT IS MEASURED: per language the coo runs the post step both ways, stdout and exit status alone and with stderr, and reports how many verdicts move and into which classes (a program's own TERMINAL text, an engine diagnostic, a note SCRIP alone prints); the cio brings that table to Lon and the stderr comparison becomes part of the grade by his word, so no suite turns red by a rule nobody measured. (7) THE ORDER inside a language, extending CIO-27 (11): absorb the loose files, regroup into ONE and MULTI.md (a pure change of form, the read-back identical), re-cut the answers with the streams apart, then the SCRIP switches and the post step. The rows are the coo's: the format-2 row gains the args and stderr fences; tests-recut-every-answer-is-cut-again-from-the-oracle-with-stdout-stderr-and-exit-status-apart is new.

- **CIO-31 (2026-11-10 07:45 CST, cio) -- NO FORK: THE TWO SWITCHES RUN EVERY TEST IN ONE PROCESS AND RESET SCRIP TO ITS WARM POINT BETWEEN TESTS, THE HEAP AND STACK BY MMAP, THE STATIC DATA FROM A SNAPSHOT; AN ABNORMAL END IS FOLLOWED BY SCRIP RE-EXECUTING ITSELF; CIO-26 (a) AND (e) AND CIO-27 (1) AND (3) ARE AMENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "It handles stdin, stdout, and stderr and a total reset of stack/heap using MMAP between each run." then "Do not use FORK."), after the cio recommended keeping the fork. MEASURED 07:4x on SCRIP 4d89b1093, the grader root's binary: the present engine runs 200 one-line SNOBOL4 rows with 200 clone calls at 225 microseconds each under strace, of 3.4 ms a row unstraced (500 rows, 1.72 s), so the fork is under a tenth of a row and the rest is each child's cold start and run; libscrip_rt.so carries 5.71 MB of data and 0.24 MB of bss, scrip 4.5 KB; the source has 87 exit and 19 _exit call sites, 169 abort sites, 4 pthread_create sites and 17 fork, system or popen sites in the runtime; the runtime maps its heap, its stack, its pinned region and its global window by anonymous mmap at start (gc_heap.c, rt_stack_overflow.c, rt.c). So dropping the fork saves little time by itself and what a fork gave for nothing must now be done by SCRIP: this ruling says how, and what makes it fast is the warm point, not the missing fork. THE DESIGN. (1) ONE PROCESS, A WARM POINT: scrip --run-one-liners and --run-multi-liners read the group and the index, then take the warm point before the first test: the list of the process's mappings, a copy of every writable mapping's bytes (the static data of scrip, libscrip_rt.so and the C library, about 6 MB measured, and the C heap up to its break), the open descriptors, the signal handlers and mask, the limits, the environment, the directory. The runner's own state (its place in the file, its output buffer) lives in a mapping it makes for itself and in its own frame, which the reset never touches; it writes its result lines with write on a descriptor it saved, never through stdio. (2) A TEST: the three streams are set by dup2 onto files (CIO-29), the directory by chdir, the timeout by a timer and the output bound by the file-size limit; the runner marks a return point with sigsetjmp and calls the driver's entry, the body of main made a function of its arguments. (3) THE FIVE ENDINGS all come back to the return point: exit, _exit and _Exit are wrapped at link in scrip and libscrip_rt.so (the 106 sites keep their text) and jump back as EXIT with the status; abort is wrapped and jumps back as ABORT; SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGFPE and SIGILL are caught on an alternate signal stack as CRASH with the signal, after the runtime's own stack-overflow handler has had its turn; the timer's signal is TIMEOUT or HANG by the processor share (CIO-30); SIGXFSZ is OVERFLOW. (4) THE RESET AFTER AN EXIT, in order: the streams flushed and the runner's three descriptors restored, every descriptor not open at the warm point closed, the directory, handlers, mask, limits and environment put back; every mapping not present at the warm point unmapped, which is the total reset of heap and stack by mmap, the next test's start mapping them fresh at that test's declared sizes; every writable mapping of the warm point restored to its warm bytes. After it SCRIP is, byte for byte in its writable memory, the process it was before the test. The first build restores all of it each test and measures the cost; restoring only the pages a test wrote (write faults caught on protected pages) is the second step, taken only if the measurement asks for it. (5) AFTER ANY OTHER ENDING, AND WHEN A TEST LEFT A THREAD OR A CHILD PROCESS ALIVE, THE RUNNER DOES NOT TRUST AN IN-PROCESS RESET: a test cut at an arbitrary instruction can hold a lock inside the C library, and a live thread cannot be unmade from inside. It writes the test's line, kills the test's child processes, and RE-EXECUTES ITSELF with execve on its own binary and a resume index: that is not a fork, it makes no process, and the kernel replaces the whole image and ends every other thread. These endings are rare and cost one image load each. A run the kernel kills outright (out of memory, SIGKILL) ends there; the post step sees which test has no line and starts the runner again after it, charging that test KILLED. (6) scrip --harness and scrip_harness_row keep their fork untouched: they are the old road, removed with it once the census is green. (7) THE PROOF grows three arms beside the same-verdict census: ORDER, the group run forward, reversed and shuffled writes byte-identical files for every test; LEAK, after a thousand tests the runner's mappings, descriptors and resident memory are those after one; ENDINGS, an exit status, an abort, a segfault, a busy loop, a blocked read, a flood of output and a test that starts a thread each give their type and the next test passes. Then the seconds against the forked engine's 3.4 ms a row on the same box: the number that says what no fork bought. (8) NEXT, NOT IN THIS ROW: the warm point taken after a language's shared start (the Prolog base prelude, 4.37 G instructions a program today), which is where a warm process pays most. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew; SKILLS scrip-architecture, scrip-c-code, scrip-build, runtime-calls, scrip-memory-model.
- **CIO-30 (2026-11-10 07:42 CST, cio) -- SCRIP REPORTS HOW EACH TEST ENDED AS A RESULT TYPE: EXIT, ABORT, CRASH, TIMEOUT, HANG, OVERFLOW, REFUSED, DEFERRED; CIO-29 (3) IS MADE EXACT.** THE WORD (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim: "Do have SCRIP report result-type, ABORT, CRASH, HANG, and TIMEOUT, etc."). MEASURED on SCRIP 4d89b1093: the runner today knows three endings, RAN, HANG (its timeout fired) and CRASH (any signal); SCRIP's source has 169 abort() sites (rt_bomb and the like), so a death by its own abort is a real class the word CRASH hides; wait4 hands the parent the child's processor seconds beside the wall seconds; the Python grader kills a test whose output passes 256 MB (S4E_OUTPUT_CAP_MB) and the runner has no such bound; the progress store's outcome words already hold ABORT, CRASH and HANG and read TIMEOUT as HANG. THE RULING: the run's line, one a test, tab separated, is the entry name, the RESULT TYPE, a number, the wall seconds, the processor seconds, the peak memory in KB. THE TYPES, each decided by the runner from what it alone observes, never from the test's output: EXIT, the program ran to an exit, the number its exit status; ABORT, it died on SIGABRT, SCRIP's own abort; CRASH, it died on any other signal the runner did not send, the number that signal; TIMEOUT, the runner killed it at timeout_s while it was working, its processor seconds at least a tenth of the wall seconds; HANG, the runner killed it at timeout_s while it was idle, under a tenth, blocked on a read, a lock or a sleep; OVERFLOW, its stdout or stderr passed the output bound (S4E_OUTPUT_CAP_MB, 256 when unset, set by the runner as the test's file-size limit) and the kernel stopped it; REFUSED, the runner could not start it (its directory, its files or the fork), the number the errno; DEFERRED, a staged test the runner does not run. For the four killed types the number is the signal. A type is never PASS or FAIL: the runner compares nothing (CIO-29). THE POST STEP reads the type first: only an EXIT is compared with the answer; ABORT, CRASH, TIMEOUT, HANG and OVERFLOW are the test's outcome as they stand, written to the progress store under its own words, TIMEOUT as HANG with the word in the note until the store's vocabulary gains TIMEOUT, which is the coo's one line in util_progress_append.py; REFUSED is could not measure, rc 2, never a red. The row is the coo's: scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew; its gate grows an arm for each type: an exit status, an abort, a segfault, a busy loop, a blocked read, a flood of output.
- **CIO-29 (2026-11-10 07:41 CST, cio) -- SCRIP IS THE RUNNER AND NOTHING MORE: IT READS THE TESTS, RUNS EACH ANEW WITH ITS STDIN, WRITES ITS STDOUT AND STDERR, AND ENFORCES THE TIMEOUT; IT COMPARES NO ANSWER AND WRITES NO RECORD; COMPARING AND RECORDING ARE THE POST STEP; CIO-26 (c), CIO-27 (6) AND (7) AND CIO-28 (1) ARE AMENDED.** THE WORDS (Lon 2026-11-10 07:4x CST in the cio's window, verbatim, in order: "Do not have SCRIP write the record.
