(1) WHAT SCRIP DOES under scrip --run-one-liners DIR --out OUT and scrip --run-multi-liners DIR --out OUT: reads the tests from ONE.EXT or MULTI.md and their attributes from ALL.csv (CIO-27 (2) to (5), unchanged); for each direct test forks, runs the program from its private directory with its stdin block on standard input, its stdout and stderr written to OUT under the entry name with slashes as underscores (NAME.out and NAME.err; one file, NAME.out, when the index says stderr is merged); enforces the test's timeout_s from the index, 120 when the cell is empty, killing the test's whole process group when it passes. (4) THE EXIT CODE no longer speaks of answers: 0 when every selected test was run or deferred, whatever it printed; 2 refused (a file, a section or an --only name it cannot read), before any test runs. Comparing every test in one Python process over files already on disk is the cheap part; the 73 ms an entry of today's road is the staging and the launches, which are gone. (6) RUNNING IS FREE, RECORDING IS THE COO'S (CIO-28 (3) stands): any seat may run either switch; the post step alone is the board. (7) THE PROOF (CIO-27 (10)) reads the same: every entry's verdict by the switches plus the post step equals its verdict by today's road, then the seconds of both. (4) scrip --harness keeps its --record as it is; it is the old road and is retired with it. (5) THE ARGUMENTS of a direct test are exactly today's row for the same entry: mode, the compile_args cell's tokens, -dNk and -sNk from heap_kb and stack_kb, the source, then two dashes and the run_args tokens; timeout_s from the index, 120 when empty, the Python grader's default. (8) MODE 4: --compile before either switch writes each direct test's assembly into --out DIR as --compile --harness does and compares nothing; the full mode-4 road stays the Python grader's until Lon lifts mode 3 only. MEASURED on SCRIP 4d89b1093: the engine exists. scrip --harness LIST (scrip.c, scrip_harness_cli and scrip_harness_main; DESIGN-PLAN batch-grader, CTO-515 and CTO-522) runs every row of a keyed list in one process, each row a fork from the warm parent with its own stdin, stdout, stderr, timeout and directory, and writes a record row (rc; RAN, HANG, CRASH or REFUSED; seconds; peak memory); a fatal, a hang or a crash is that row's record and the list goes on. What it does not do is read a container: the Python harness extracts every entry to a file on disk, writes the list, and reads every stdout file back to grade it. By that road lap 9 graded, in mode 3, sno-rungs 2211 entries in 161 s, raku-rungs 1390 in 128 s, icn-rungs 956 in 65 s, pas-rungs 385 in 56 s, snc-rungs 358 in 28 s and pl-rungs 584 in 439 s (CYCLE.log); the gain of the direct road is not measured and is the row's step 0. THE RULING. (a) TWO SWITCHES, Lon's names: scrip --run-one-liners DIR reads ONE.EXT and ONE.stdout of a container directory; scrip --run-multi-liners DIR reads MULTI.md (CIO-24 f, CIO-25); --compile before either is mode 4, as --harness has it. Each is the --harness engine with a reader in front: for every test a fork from the warm parent, the program text handed over from memory under the name ENTRY.EXT, its stdin block on standard input, nothing staged on disk. (b) THE ATTRIBUTES come from ALL.csv beside the files, read by column name: heap_kb, stack_kb, timeout_s, compile_args, run_args, cwd; a column the table lacks is the default. (c) THE COMPARISON IS IN SCRIP AND IS BYTE FOR BYTE: the parent captures the child's stdout and compares it with the test's stdout block; the record row gains SAME or DIFFERS. That is not yet the grade: a test graded through a mask, a compare token, a nonzero want_rc, merged stderr or written files is decided by the Python grader, so with --out DIR scrip writes the actual stdout and stderr of every test that is not RAN, SAME and rc 0, and of no other; the grader re-grades only those. A passing test costs no file and no Python. (d) SELECTION: --only FILE names the entries of one cycle cell; without it the whole group runs. Exit 0 when every selected test is RAN, SAME, rc 0; 1 otherwise; 2 refused with the file or section named. (e) A TEST NEVER SEES THE ONE BEFORE IT: the fork is the reset, as batch-grader step 2 proved it; the warm parent holds only what every test of the file shares (the runtime, and for Prolog the base prelude, batch-grader step 8, where an empty program costs 4.37 G instructions today). (f) THE CYCLE: a cell of a container in format 2 is two launches, one a group, in place of one launch of corpus_suite_harness.py over staged files; the harness keeps the selection, the record's reading, the re-grade of the DIFFERS rows and the progress rows. (g) WHO: the SCRIP side is the cto's design and row, scrip-run-one-liners-and-run-multi-liners-read-the-container-files-and-run-each-test-anew (minted, owner cto, FREE); the cto is idle under DUO and a telegram wakes nobody, so the cio asked Lon for the word to add the cto to the live set. The harness side is the coo's, after it. ORDER: format 2 lands first on one master (rebus, 43 entries) so the switches have a file to read; the two then go forward together.

MEASURED 07:3x on corpus f141ec484 and SCRIP 4d89b1093: (1) a Scriptix file is fenced blocks tagged with the language (SNOBOL4, Icon, Prolog, Raku, Pascal, Snocone, Rebus, SCRIP; polyglot.c), and scrip runs one .md as ONE polyglot program, every block linked with the others (tests/scrip_test/cross_lang.md: a SNOBOL4 block calls an Icon and a Prolog block); (2) three backticks occur on 6 lines of all the containers, none in the seven masters: rosetta-prolog (3 program lines, 2 answer lines) and xsb_tests (1 line, which holds four); (3) polyglot.c finds the opening and the closing fence with strstr anywhere in the text, not at a line start; (4) the multi-line entries sit in hundreds of families of one or two entries (snobol4 518 families for 1400 entries), so a file per family is 518 files; (5) the largest master is 1.13 MB of programs and 0.44 MB of answers (snobol4). THE RULING, replacing MULTI.EXT, MULTI.stdout and MULTI.stdin of CIO-24 (f); everything else of CIO-24 stands, the one-liner group included (ONE.EXT and ONE.stdout, line for line). (a) THE FILE: MULTI.md in the container directory. A test is ONE SECTION: a heading line of two hashes and the entry's name exactly as ALL.csv has it; then the program in a fence tagged with the language as Scriptix spells it; then, when the test has input, a fence tagged stdin; then a fence tagged stdout. A test's three parts sit together, so no file is aligned with another by position, a reader sees the program beside its answer, and a viewer highlights it. Text between a heading and its first fence is the test's own description, kept and never graded. (b) THE FENCE is a line that is only backticks and the tag: three, or one more than the longest run of backticks inside the block (Markdown's own rule), so the 6 lines are held without escaping; the reader and writer are line-anchored. A block's bytes run from the line after the opening fence to the newline before the closing fence; a program or an answer whose last line has no newline is told by the index (a column the converter writes only where it is so), since the read-back must be byte for byte. (c) IT IS SCRIPTIX SYNTAX, NOT ONE SCRIPTIX PROGRAM: the harness extracts a section and runs its program as it runs an entry today; scrip is never handed MULTI.md whole. A section may hold more than one language fence, and is then one polyglot test run as a .md: that is the home Scriptix's own tests never had under corpus/tests. polyglot.c's strstr fence is SCRIP's and the cto's: told, not changed here. (d) SIZE AND VIEWING: one MULTI.md a container; when the coo measures that the largest does not render where Lon views it, the file is cut into MULTI.01.md, MULTI.02.md and so on in index order at a fixed count of tests, new tests appended to the last; never a file per family. (e) THE INDEX AND THE TABLES do not change: ALL.csv with its group column, PARTITION.tsv, ALL.wantrc, ALL.argv, ALL.mask. (f) THE MOVE is CIO-24 (f)'s: the reader takes a container directory in any of the formats, regroup converts one directory and refuses unless the read-back gives the same entries in order with the same program, answer and stdin bytes, the masters first. The row is the coo's, re-pointed by this ruling: tests-layout-the-container-is-two-groups-one-liner-and-multi-liner-with-stdin-and-stdout-named.

THE RULING: (1) under the one launch a swi-cases entry runs as THREE FILES: the shim corpus/tests/prolog/plunit.pl, the entry's ORIGIN file (<companion_dir>/<cwd>/<basename>.pl, real path, real lines, real directory) and a two-line wrapper the harness writes in the staging directory; mode 4 compiles the same three; the --source-path switch (CIO-20) is not used on this road and stays for other entries with a cwd. (2) The container is the RECORD and the origin is the FILE: the launch refuses rc 2 when the origin is missing or when the container's entry text does not contain the origin's bytes (a stale container is rebuilt, never graded). (3) The container's entry text becomes the test file alone (CIO-15 (c) revised): the record equals the file byte for byte; the ref stays beside and verbatim. THE ROWS: the mechanism (1) to (3) with its probe is fleet-worktree-sweep-reclaims-landed-escalated-and-unnamed-worktrees-and-saves-the-unlanded-patch, rank 1 in tier-haiku (junior_09 woken; its own dead worktree is the first it removes); the cap line (4) the cio lands tonight under fleet-worktree-cap-default-40. A seat's own dead worktree it removes itself with rm --discard once its row is DONE or has left it; a seat removes no other seat's. The hand removal of other seats' worktrees was refused to the cio by its permission classifier and is not retried: the sweep removes as the owner, and that is the one road.
- **CIO-16 (2026-11-09 02:41 CST, cio) -- A REJECTION TEST IS AN EXPECTED REFUSAL, NOT NO_ORACLE_ANSWER: THE PAT CONTAINER KEEPS ITS 428 THROUGH THE ONE GRADER'S ISO_EXPECTED_REFUSALS ROAD, THE TABLE'S SECOND COLUMN NAMING THE LINE OR CLAUSE THE REFUSAL MUST NAME; NO NEW GRADER ATTRIBUTE.** THE ASK (officer_04 through the coo, 2026-11-09 02:2x CST, q-pascal-pat-container-in-the-one-layout-1): PAT ships 428 programs, 427 iso7185prt rejection tests the ISO 7185 suite expects a compiler to refuse; the package builder absorbs only the programs fpc compiles, so junior_07's rebuild keeps 37 and the denominator would fall from 428. MEASURED BY THE CIO: corpus_suite_harness.py already grades an expected refusal from ISO_EXPECTED_REFUSALS.tsv beside the container (R-1228, R-1657; fpc_tests carries 19 rows, rosetta-pascal its own): PASS in a mode when the mode refuses with the language's diagnostic, the ref never compared, the denominator unchanged. The deleted test_pascal_pat_suite.sh graded a rejection test PASS only when the refusal NAMED ITS REASON: the source line Pascal-P5's .ecp marks (within one) or an ISO 7185 clause; a crash or a hang is never a refusal (the coo 2026-11-05); a test with no marked line and no clause was EXCLUDED NO_ORACLE_ANSWER (R-1696). SCRIP today refuses iso7185prt0001 with "pascal parse error line 9: syntax error" and its .ecp marks line 9. THE RULING: (1) a rejection test whose refusal can be checked against a known line or clause is not a program with no oracle answer: the ISO suite and the P5 transcript are its answer; it stays in the denominator as an expected refusal, never asked of fpc for a ref. (2) The mechanism is the existing road, extended: the builder absorbs the entry with an empty ref and writes its row of ISO_EXPECTED_REFUSALS.tsv with the second column "line N ..." from the .ecp marks or "clause X ..." from the header, else excludes it NO_ORACLE_ANSWER; the harness's expected-refusal verdict reads that column (a named line within one, or any ISO 7185 clause, over stdout and stderr joined) and the exit classes (124 and 137 hang, 129 to 159 crash, any other nonzero exit with the named reason a refusal). (3) ISO_ACCEPTS.tsv's ten programs take the ordinary fpc road (R-1269); EXCLUDED.tsv and UNGRADABLE.tsv stand by name. (4) junior_07's 37-entry rebuild does not land: a container that narrows a denominator is refused as the old runner's header refused it. The answer on the ask names the three Haiku rows for officer_04 to cut. NOT DONE by the cio: nothing changed; the ask answered and the parent unblocked.
- **CIO-15 (2026-11-09 02:02 CST, cio) -- THE ONE GRADER GRADES A PER-CASE PACKAGE (SWI, LOGTALK) BY A GRADER ATTRIBUTE THAT EXPANDS ONE ENTRY'S RUN INTO ITS CASE ROWS, AND A LIBRARY THROUGH ITS DRIVER AS AN ENTRY NAMED AFTER THE LIBRARY; A PARTITION NAMES ONLY CONTAINER ENTRIES; SWI FIRST, FIVE HAIKU ROWS.** THE ASK (the coo's telegram swi-has-no-grader-under-the-one-launch, 2026-11-09 01:3x CST, on Lon's "ONE UNIFIED test suite system" of 2026-11-08 23:0x and "Get SWI to 100%." of 23:5x): since harness cc2783502 every cell is one launch of corpus_suite_harness.py over the suite's ALL container; SWI has no container, so its cells refuse rc 2 and nothing writes an SWI reading; the same gap refuses ipl chunk:01 to 08, arizona chunk:01, gimpel chunk:01 and the eleven suites with no container. MEASURED BY THE CIO (01:4x to 01:5x CST, SCRIP db9e1f5bb, harness 08c53457): (1) a plunit file runs byte-identically as ONE source made of the shim corpus/tests/prolog/plunit.pl, the test file and the two-line wrapper main :- run_tests. :- initialization(main). (tabling/test_wfs.pl, three files against the concatenation: the same case lines), so an SWI file is one container entry in the banner-block format. (2) The score-row writer already counts SWI per case: util_score_row.py from-progress has the swi road (SUITE_ROW_SWI, _swi_case_census over util_swi_match.py's enumeration) and the live progress table carries the old runner's rows as class package, suite swi, program <file>.pl:<unit>:<test>#k, note "case of <file>.pl" (results.tsv, grader rows of 2026-11-08 20:25). So the one gap is the harness: it grades an entry by stdout equality and writes one row per entry. (3) The harness reads its attributes from ALL.csv by column name in read_command_line_columns (compile_args, run_args, out_files, stderr, compare, stage, answer, mode_gap_s) and records one progress row per entry and mode at the one place in cmd_run where _progress_rows.append runs; a column it does not know is ignored, so a builder may write the new columns before the harness reads them. (4) swi_tests has ALL.csv (162 file rows with heap_kb, stack_kb, timeout_s) and PARTITION.tsv (core, chunk:01, chunk:02) but no ALL.pl and no ALL.ref; every file's oracle verdicts sit beside it as <file>.ref. (5) The IPL refusal is not a missing driver road but a stale partition: ipl's ALL.csv has 851 rows, ALL.excluded.txt 773 of them, ALL.icn 78 entries, and util_suite_partition.py builds the chunks over every ALL.csv row, so chunk:01 to 08 name excluded programs the container does not hold (the harness refuses "N of its entries are not in"); arizona (135 rows, 107 entries, 41 excluded, 37 of them drivers that load C through .clib) and gimpel (145 rows, 144 entries) refuse the same way. (6) ipl ships 642 refs beside its sources and 362 NAME_driver.icn files; the deleted runner graded a library through its driver under the library's name (R-1269) with the driver's directory on IPATH; ipl has no CONTRACT.tsv, so the builder absorbed the 78 programs only. THE RULING. (a) THE GRADER ATTRIBUTE: ALL.csv gains the column grader, empty or text (today's stdout equality), swi-cases or logtalk-cases; for a cases grader the harness runs the entry once per mode as today, hands its stdout, exit status, the entry's ref block and the entry's origin source to the package's matcher (util_swi_match.py; util_logtalk_grade.py's verdict for Logtalk), and records ONE PROGRESS ROW PER CASE named <entry>.pl:<case key> with the matcher's word (PASS, FAIL, UNGRADED, CRASH, HANG are all in util_progress_append's vocabulary) and the note "case of <entry>.pl", counts each case in the board tallies, and prints one line per entry (SWI_CASES <entry> cases=N hit=H miss=M ungraded=U); the entry's own row is not written; the entry reads red when any case misses. The suite row then comes from the writer's existing swi road, unchanged. (b) THE CWD ATTRIBUTE: ALL.csv gains the column cwd, a directory under the container's; when set, the harness runs the entry (both modes) from that directory with the staged source by absolute path, because plunit files open data beside them (files/test_glob.pl) and the deleted runner ran each file from its own directory. (c) THE SWI CONTAINER: CONTRACT.tsv beside swi_tests declares units plunit; the builder's plunit mode makes one entry per .pl that declares begin_tests and has a .ref beside it (CONTAINERS.tsv files skipped; a .pl without a .ref excluded by name), its text the shim, the file and the wrapper, its ALL.ref block the beside .ref verbatim, its ALL.csv row the existing attributes plus grader swi-cases and cwd its directory; PARTITION.tsv is rebuilt and stays three cells. (d) LOGTALK: the same hook with grader logtalk-cases over a container of one entry per case (its ALL.csv already lists 3617 cases) whose text is util_logtalk_grade.program_text's program and whose ref block carries the case's want (succ, fail or the ball term) so verdict() decides from the run's outcome; a design the row measures first on one group. (e) A PARTITION NAMES ONLY CONTAINER ENTRIES: util_suite_partition.py build drops the rows ALL.excluded.txt and CONTAINERS.tsv name, so the harness's partition refusal cannot fire on a consistent package; the three partitions are rebuilt. (f) A LIBRARY THROUGH ITS DRIVER: ipl gets CONTRACT.tsv units ref-beside and ipath procs and gprocs (the key repeated, the builder joining them), the builder absorbs NAME_driver.icn with NAME_driver.ref as entry NAME (R-1269: the driver runs, the library is counted), and the harness exports IPATH from the container's CONTRACT.tsv ipath rows into the run and compile environment of every entry of that container, nothing else; arizona's 37 .clib drivers stay excluded by their named reason. (g) THE ELEVEN CONTAINERLESS STDOUT SUITES (snoflake dotnet testpgms inria fpc pat roast x64tests x32tests and the two above) are builds, not designs: util_build_package_suite.py <pkg> --lang L per package, each a row the coo or officer_08 (the records and cycle owner, CIO-12) mints with the builder's refusal as its measurement; the cio minted none of them. THE ROWS, tier-haiku, SWI first: suite-swi-container-one-plunit-entry-per-file-built-by-the-package-builder-under-contract-units-plunit (c, with b's column written); suite-harness-grader-attribute-swi-cases-expands-an-entry-into-its-case-rows-and-cwd-runs-it-from-its-directory (a and b; BLOCKED-ON the container); suite-harness-grader-attribute-logtalk-cases-and-the-logtalk-container-of-one-entry-per-case (d; BLOCKED-ON the grader hook); suite-partition-build-drops-the-excluded-and-container-rows-so-every-partition-entry-is-a-container-entry (e); suite-ipl-container-absorbs-the-driver-graded-libraries-under-contract-units-ref-beside-and-ipath (f; after e). WHEN the first two land, the cycle's next lap grades swi core and its two chunks and the writer's swi road rewrites the SWI row; the coo reads it. NOT DONE by the cio: no file changed; the rows carry the measurements, the exact columns, the call sites and the proofs.
- **CIO-14 (2026-11-09 01:46 CST, cio) -- THE THREE SWI DESIGNS ESCALATED TO TIER-FABLE ARE SPLIT BETWEEN THE TWO FABLE SEATS; THE CIO'S DESIGN OF THE TABLING-WFS ROW (THREE MEASURED CAUSES, THREE HAIKU ROWS) AND OF THE GLOB ROW (THE DEFERRED IF BRANCH INSIDE A UNIT JOINS THE OPEN UNIT); THE ROUTING RULE FOR THE COO.** LON'S WORD (2026-11-08 23:5x, in the coo's window, relayed by COO-497 and the coo's escalations of 01:1x): "Get SWI to 100%." THE SPLIT (01:1x CST): the coo escalated four SWI designs to tier-fable in one hour (strings, tabling-wfs, files-glob, test_reload) with "pass it to the cto if his"; both Fable seats are alive, so the cio took tabling-wfs (job 6235) and files-glob (6238) and assigned strings (6241, seat-cto) and reload (tier-fable) to the cto beside his prolog_clause row. THE ROUTING RULE from here, so the coo asks nobody per item: a tier-fable SWI design about the reader, the lowerer, the runtime, the clause database or module resolution wakes the CTO; one about the tabling family (tabling.pl, WFS, moded and incremental tables) or the grading and plunit shim shape wakes the CIO. THE TABLING-WFS DESIGN, every cause MEASURED by the cio 01:2x to 01:4x on SCRIP db9e1f5bb with probes in /home/resources/postoffice/escalations/probes/pl_tabling_cio14_*.pl against swipl 9.0.4: (a) dwin#1: both move orders lose the delay (dw.pl and dw2.pl print true, dw3.pl prints 2-true where swipl prints 2-win_cnt(0,_) and dwin(0)): the conditional contribution to a moded aggregate always completes LAST because its negative consumer parks, and the prelude's '$tbl_sl_mins'/5 writes the delay list mu(M,D) only when the answer skeleton is new (E==0); a later conditional contribution goes through '$tbl_mput'/6 which keeps the old delays or none. CURE: a contribution that changes the aggregate appends its delays to the entry's delays (the entry becomes mu(New, Old ++ D)); an unconditional contribution with the same value as a conditional entry strips the delays; the scheduler's unconditional count ('$tbl_sl' op 7) is flagged only when the written entry is unconditional; the entry is replaced by delete and insert so a parked consumer reading by slot sees it. Row prolog-tabling-a-moded-answer-keeps-the-delays-of-every-contribution-that-changed-it. (b) as#1: NOT a moded fault. The plain table puas(1,X) :- not_exists(puas(1,X)) gives the same empty residual (b1.pl), and the structure p :- tnot(q), q :- p works (b9, b10, b11), as does a program-defined tabled wrapper tc(G) :- call(G) in place of tabled_call (b16). The fault is the prelude's tabled_call/1: '$tnot_impl'/2 resolves the implementation by current_predicate('$tbl tabled_call'/1), which is false from the user module (b17.pl prints tc_no, impl_fail), so tnot/1 falls off the SL scheduler onto the old engine's tnot road, which succeeds with no delay (b14 prints _G0-true and an empty residual; b15, the ground form, fails outright). CURE: one clause, '$tnot_impl'(tabled_call(G),'$tbl tabled_call'(G)):-!., beside the existing undefined/0 clause. Row prolog-tabling-tnot-resolves-the-preludes-tabled-call-so-not-exists-of-a-non-ground-goal-delays. MEASURED beside it and left as is: swipl answers a moded aggregate whose last contribution was unconditional as unconditional (b4.pl: 5-true where SCRIP keeps the delay); no suite case reads it. (c) reeval#1: the probe reeval.pl agrees with swipl on 17 of 18 lines; the last differs: after assertz(d(0)) and the re-evaluation of p to the same answer set [1], swipl reads p2 valid (the manual, tabling-incremental: an unchanged re-evaluation decrements the invalid count of the affected tables) and SCRIP reads it invalid, because '$tbl_valid'/4 drops an invalid table and re-evaluates into a fresh trie with no comparison and no word to the dependents. CURE in tabling.pl alone: '$tbl_valid' hands the old trie as old(T0); '$tbl_enter'(old(T0),...) creates the fresh table with the idg's Forced field carrying old(T0) for an incremental table (the field is never read for incr; mono and lazy keep false); at completion ('$tbl_complete', '$tbl_done', '$tbl_fin_all') a hook '$tbl_cmpl'/1 compares the two answer sets by '$tbl_chg'/3: equal, the dependents' Deps are rebound from T0 to T and every dependent that is invalid, not dirty, and whose every table dependency is complete is set complete again, recursively; changed, the dependents are rebound and marked dirty (Forced=dirty, cleared when they re-evaluate); T0 is destroyed. The SL road ('$tbl_sl_get', '$tbl_sl_nget') maps old(_) to none. The dirty mark is the falsecount: a dependent is valid again only when no dependency is invalid and none changed since its last evaluation. Row prolog-tabling-an-unchanged-incremental-re-evaluation-revalidates-its-dependents. THE GLOB DESIGN (2), MEASURED on the probe pl_deferred_if_in_unit_u1.pl: swipl runs both tests; SCRIP prints a permission_error(modify,static_procedure,test/1) warning, the unit banner twice and runs test a twice, because '$cc_consult'/3 (the consult prelude in prolog_parse.c, line 3164) wraps the deferred branch in a fresh begin_tests/end_tests (the shim's begin_tests adds the suite a second time) and feeds it to '$consult_term', which asserts test(b) against the compiled unit's static test/1. CURE: a branch inside a unit is read by the unit loader road the load_files prelude already has ('$load_items'/3, which turns test/1 and test/2 clauses into pj_test/4 facts qualified by plunit_U), with no begin_tests wrapper; and '$load_one' (line 3957) stops asserting the test clause itself (pj_test is what run_tests reads). Row prolog-plunit-a-deferred-if-branch-inside-a-unit-joins-the-open-unit-through-the-unit-loader; the cyrillic and file_names reds under the :- if are its acceptance. - **CIO-10 (2026-11-08 11:37 CST, cio) -- CIO-10 (2026-11-08 11:4x CST, cio) -- THE OFFICERS DESIGN; THE CIO AND THE CTO SLEEP UNTIL AN ASK OR AN ESCALATION REACHES TIER-FABLE; A FLEET DEFECT GOES TO AN OFFICER, NEVER TO THE CIO FIRST; THE NEW TEAM KEYED; THE LAST FOUR CURES LANDED.** LON'S WORD (in the cio's window, 11:3x to 11:4x, verbatim): "I really want to see these Fable seats sleeping. Finish design and sleep. Give more to officers." / "CIO and CTO need to slow down and delegate more to officers." / "So then the system is out of whack and depends on the CTO/CIO WAY TOO much. The officers must step up for design. Hello." / "Now!!!" THE RULE: (1) An officer (Opus) designs: a fleet defect with its measurement goes by telegram to officer_05 (the bus, the queue, the lander, the cycle, the records; its row is CIO-8 phase two) or to the UniTests officer whose step it touches, and that officer designs the cure, cuts it under THE HAIKU BAR and queues it; the coo routes a defect to the officer, never to the cio. (2) The cio and the cto are reached only by ask (ask-fable) or by an escalation that reaches tier-fable, and only after the officer has written its own proposal in the ask so that a yes settles it (officer_05's eight questions of 11:0x are the model). (3) The cio answers an ask with the answer verb and goes back to sleep; it lands code only on a fleet question no officer can answer. (4) The cto the same for SCRIP. LANDED SINCE CIO-9 (harness 710f57f1e, 0a52a1a5e): next syncs all four repos and runs make -j8 (a cache fetch) before it reads a premise, refusing when make fails; a RED premise at one root PARKS the row PARKED-PREMISE-RED:<reader>@<scrip tree> with the reader's four trees in the LEDGER, it no longer retires it (three false SUPERSEDED today; the designer re-reads on origin, recuts if wrong, queues again); the answer verb releases the identity before its done leg (the cto's three refusals); the refused-head probe names junior_20 and is the landing's gate; util_score_row.py from-progress grades a row in m3 alone under S4E_MODES=m3, which the cycle exports, so the UniTests headline reads 110 of 163, the m3 count, not the blended 105 (the coo 11:29). ANSWERED, NO CODE: (a) the ONE RUNNER lane (the coo 11:25): a UniTests verdict by hand is taken as seat grader exactly as CIO-7 wrote it (S4E_HOME=/home/claude_grader S4E_SEAT=grader S4E_CLASS=grader S4E_BATCH=1 S4E_CHUNK=entry:<name> bash harness/scripts/test_icon_unicon_suite.sh, mode 3); a roster seat's reading never reaches results.tsv by design, and the lane stays icon=coo; the cycle grades the whole package each lap. (b) The two-jobs-one-row class (officer_04 12:1x, unclaim then queue; officer_03 11:03, park then queue): phase two, officer_05's DESIGN-PLAN bus-one-queue, no patch before it. (c) The fleet view's "(no root, retiring)" for every numbered seat: a stale root map in s4e_msg.sh fleet; officer_05's plan, one Haiku row. THE TEAM KEYED (the coo 11:33, Lon: "How about throwing 8 more juniors at some of the remaining 58. Let's see what volume can do." / "Hire more officers. What's the problem?"): MODE's FLEET line names junior_01 to junior_15 and officer_01 to officer_08; fourteen juniors at Haiku high; UniTests officers 01 to 04 and 06 to 08; officer_05 on the bus. ACCEPTANCE stays CIO-8's, read by the coo at 15:00, plus one line: asks that reached the cio today after this ruling, and whether each carried the officer's proposal.

- **CIO-8 (2026-11-08 10:50 CST, cio) -- CIO-8 (2026-11-08 10:4x CST, cio) -- THE FLEET SYSTEM, DESIGNED ON LON'S LAW: DESIGN FLOWS DOWN, PROBLEMS FLOW UP; THE SEAT THAT MOVES WORK WAKES ITS TAKER; NOBODY WAITS ON THE COO; THE DOORS WIDENED AND THE CYCLE FOUR WIDE ON THE READINGS.** LON'S WORD (in the cio's window, 10:3x to 10:4x, verbatim): "Scrutinize how COO is orchestrating the fleet. Why is not the fleet escalating up and design assigning down on their own accord? Do each every action depend on COO?" / "Have you designed a system or a whack a mole game? Which?" / "Why are things moving so slowly. I do have 16 cores and fast machine." / "Will you be fixing your stupid design or just sitting on your ass? Which?" / "DESIGN FLOWS DOWN, and PROBLEMS FLOW UP. Design goes Fable -> Opus -> Sonnet -> Haiku, and PROBLEMS go Haiku -> Sonnet -> Opus -> Fable. Does anybody NOT KNOW they failed? Does anybody NOT KNOW they have finished a design. Does COO not available for EMERGENCIES, NOT babysitting." / "So get your act together and design a fleet system. Like now." THE ANSWER: a whack-a-mole game, MEASURED at 10:3x. Every wake depended on the coo (the Stop gate holds nobody since Lon's 08:1x word, so a stopped seat waits for a SendMessage; the coo sent none today and was grading a suite by hand); tier-haiku held 0 ready jobs and 171 buried while six juniors sat idle since 10:18; the officers queued their UniTests cuts to tier-sonnet, not tier-haiku (5 rows QUEUED:tier-sonnet); senior_01 escalated in prose in a LEDGER and tier-opus held 0 ready; one ask sat unanswered in ask-fable all morning with 5 jobs ready in seat-cto; the coo's own LEDGER line of 10:26 names five breaks in the first hour and CIO-6 five the morning before. The disease: three stores of one fact (the QUEUE.tsv state, the beanstalkd job, the baton) kept in step by about thirty verbs each with its own rule, and no push: a Claude session has no inbound event but a SendMessage from another session. THE SLOWNESS, MEASURED (10:35; SLOT_WAITS.tsv, CYCLE.log, cgroup cpu.stat): load 8 on 16 cores, CPU PSI 0, no cgroup throttled; 524 slot waits of 12975 s since 06:00, of them 236 by the lander's table checks (4413 s; 14 checks, three at a time behind a pool of 8 shared by 14 seats; waits of 260 s) and 12 landing admissions (1491 s; two landings at once); the cycle one process, 20 cells in 1579 s, 78 s a cell, a lap of 190 cells 250 min; the juniors 40 to 80 CPU-seconds each in three hours (idle), the officers 500 to 750 (working). THE LAW OF THE SYSTEM (replaces the wake rule of CIO-7; the ladder of COO-481 stands): (1) DESIGN FLOWS DOWN: the cio or the cto designs; an officer (Opus) cuts the design into rows under THE HAIKU BAR and queues each to tier-haiku; the officer who finishes a step has finished when its rows are in the tube and a junior is woken. (2) PROBLEMS FLOW UP: a junior that fails after one honest try runs escalate with the measurement; the job moves to tier-sonnet and a senior is woken; a senior that fails escalates to tier-opus and an officer is woken; an officer that fails escalates to tier-fable and the cio or the cto is woken; at the top the row parks for Lon. The one who failed knows it; nobody else has to notice. The mover sends that SendMessage before it does anything else. A seat with nothing served runs next --wait 240 again while MODE names it live: it waits on the tube, it never sits idle. The Stop hook holds nobody (Lon 08:1x stands). (4) THE COO IS FOR EMERGENCIES: it grades through the cycle, routes a new red to its designer, answers a fleet defect with its measurement to the cio; it wakes nobody as a routine and runs no suite by hand that the cycle runs.
